[Previous
Version](section-5-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Term of Copyright]{.HTitleText2} {#h-102776 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of copyright

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
expressly provided by this Act, the term for which copyright subsists is
the life of the author, the remainder of the calendar year in which the
author dies, and a period of 70 years following the end of that calendar
year.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 6
- 1993, c. 44, s. 58
- [2022, c. 10, s. 276](#1357394-1388222){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357394-1388222.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 6"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Anonymous and pseudonymous works

- **[[6.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided in section 6.2
  and in subsection (2), where the identity of the author of a work is
  unknown, copyright in the work shall subsist until the end of 75 years
  following the end of the calendar year in which the work is made.
  However, if the work is published before the copyright expires, the
  copyright continues until the earlier of the end of 75 years following
  the end of the calendar year in which the first publication occurs and
  100 years following the end of the calendar year in which the work was
  made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity of author commonly known

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, during any term referred to in subsection (1),
  the author's identity becomes commonly known, the term provided in
  section 6 applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 58
- [2020, c. 1, s. 24](#1237526-1253458){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237526-1253458.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 6.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Anonymous and pseudonymous works of joint
authorship

- **[[6.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided in subsection
  (2), where the identity of all the authors of a work of joint
  authorship is unknown, copyright in the work shall subsist until the
  end of 75 years following the end of the calendar year in which the
  work is made. However, if the work is published before the copyright
  expires, the copyright continues until the earlier of the end of 75
  years following the end of the calendar year in which the first
  publication occurs and 100 years following the end of the calendar
  year in which the work was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity of author commonly known

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, during any term referred to in subsection (1),
  the identity of one or more of the authors becomes commonly known,
  copyright subsists for the life of whichever of those authors dies
  last, the remainder of the calendar year in which that author dies and
  a period of 70 years following the end of that calendar year.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 58
- [2020, c. 1, s. 24](#1237526-1253464){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237526-1253464.html"
  role="button"}
- [2022, c. 10, s. 277](#1357396-1388225){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357396-1388225.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-6.2-20200701.html "Link to previous version of section 6.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of copyright in certain posthumous
works

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to subsection (2), in the case of a literary, dramatic or musical
  work, or an engraving, in which copyright subsists at the date of the
  death of the author --- or, in the case of a work of joint authorship,
  at or immediately before the date of the death of the author who dies
  last --- but which has not been published or, in the case of a lecture
  or a dramatic or musical work, been performed in public or
  communicated to the public by telecommunication, before that date,
  copyright subsists for the longer of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the period until publication, or performance in
    public or communication to the public by telecommunication,
    whichever may first happen, as well as the remainder of the calendar
    year of the publication or of the performance in public or
    communication to the public by telecommunication, as the case may
    be, and for a period of 50 years following the end of that calendar
    year, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the life of the author --- or, in the case of a
    work of joint authorship, the life of the author who dies last ---
    as well as the remainder of the calendar year in which that author
    dies and a period of 70 years following the end of the calendar year
    in which that author dies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsection (1)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies only if the work in question
  was published or performed in public or communicated to the public by
  telecommunication, as the case may be, before December 31, 1998.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional provision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a work was not published or performed in public or
  communicated to the public by telecommunication before December 31,
  1998, if subsection (1) would apply to that work had it been published
  or performed in public or communicated to the public by
  telecommunication before that day, and if the relevant death referred
  to in subsection (1) occurred during the period of 50 years
  immediately before that day, copyright subsists in the work, whether
  or not the work is published or performed in public or communicated to
  the public by telecommunication on or after that day,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} until December 31, 2048; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the life of the author --- or, in the case of a
    work of joint authorship, the life of the author who dies last ---
    as well as the remainder of the calendar year in which that author
    dies and a period of 70 years following the end of that calendar
    year, if that period ends after December 31, 2048.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 7
- 1993, c. 44, s. 58
- 1997, c. 24, s. 6
- [2022, c. 10, s. 278](#1357398-1388237){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357398-1388237.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 7"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1993, c. 44,
s. 59\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cases of joint authorship

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** In the case of a work of
joint authorship, except as provided in section 6.2 or subsection 7(1)
or (3), copyright subsists during the life of the author who dies last,
for the remainder of the calendar year in which that author dies, and
for a period of 70 years following the end of that calendar year, and
references in this Act to the period after the expiration of any
specified number of years from the end of the calendar year of the death
of the author shall be construed as references to the period after the
expiration of the like number of years from the end of the calendar year
of the death of the author who dies last.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 9
- 1993, c. 44, s. 60
- [2020, c. 1, s. 25](#1237530-1253467){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237530-1253467.html"
  role="button"}
- [2022, c. 10, s. 279](#1357400-1388240){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357400-1388240.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9-20200701.html "Link to previous version of section 9"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 20,
s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 10"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1997, c. 24,
s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cinematographic works

**[[11.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11.1 .sectionLabel}** Except for
cinematographic works in which the arrangement or acting form or the
combination of incidents represented give the work a dramatic character,
copyright in a cinematographic work or a compilation of cinematographic
works shall subsist until the end of 70 years following the end of the
calendar year in which the cinematographic work or the compilation is
made. However, if the cinematographic work or the compilation is
published before the copyright expires, the copyright continues until
the earlier of the end of 75 years following the end of the calendar
year in which the first publication occurs and 100 years following the
end of the calendar year in which the cinematographic work or the
compilation was made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 60
- 1997, c. 24, s. 9
- [2020, c. 1, s. 26](#1237534-1253470){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237534-1253470.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 11.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where copyright belongs to Her Majesty

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** Without prejudice to any
rights or privileges of the Crown, where any work is, or has been,
prepared or published by or under the direction or control of Her
Majesty or any government department, the copyright in the work shall,
subject to any agreement with the author, belong to Her Majesty and in
that case shall continue for the remainder of the calendar year of the
first publication of the work and for a period of fifty years following
the end of that calendar year.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 12
- 1993, c. 44, s. 60
:::

### [Ownership of Copyright]{.HTitleText2} {#h-102834 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ownership of copyright

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act, the author of a
  work shall be the first owner of the copyright therein.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 20, s. 7\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work made in the course of employment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the author of a work was in the employment of
  some other person under a contract of service or apprenticeship and
  the work was made in the course of his employment by that person, the
  person by whom the author was employed shall, in the absence of any
  agreement to the contrary, be the first owner of the copyright, but
  where the work is an article or other contribution to a newspaper,
  magazine or similar periodical, there shall, in the absence of any
  agreement to the contrary, be deemed to be reserved to the author a
  right to restrain the publication of the work, otherwise than as part
  of a newspaper, magazine or similar periodical.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignments and licences

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The owner of the copyright in any work may assign the
  right, either wholly or partially, and either generally or subject to
  limitations relating to territory, medium or sector of the market or
  other limitations relating to the scope of the assignment, and either
  for the whole term of the copyright or for any other part thereof, and
  may grant any interest in the right by licence, but no assignment or
  grant is valid unless it is in writing signed by the owner of the
  right in respect of which the assignment or grant is made, or by the
  owner's duly authorized agent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ownership in case of partial assignment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where, under any partial assignment of copyright, the
  assignee becomes entitled to any right comprised in copyright, the
  assignee, with respect to the rights so assigned, and the assignor,
  with respect to the rights not assigned, shall be treated for the
  purposes of this Act as the owner of the copyright, and this Act has
  effect accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment of right of action

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, it is deemed always to have
  been the law that a right of action for infringement of copyright may
  be assigned in association with the assignment of the copyright or the
  grant of an interest in the copyright by licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusive licence

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, it is deemed always to have
  been the law that a grant of an exclusive licence in a copyright
  constitutes the grant of an interest in the copyright by licence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 13
- 1997, c. 24, s. 10
- 2012, c. 20, s. 7
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation where author is first owner of
copyright

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  the author of a work is the first owner of the copyright therein, no
  assignment of the copyright and no grant of any interest therein, made
  by him, otherwise than by will, after June 4, 1921, is operative to
  vest in the assignee or grantee any rights with respect to the
  copyright in the work beyond the expiration of twenty-five years from
  the death of the author, and the reversionary interest in the
  copyright expectant on the termination of that period shall, on the
  death of the author, notwithstanding any agreement to the contrary,
  devolve on his legal representatives as part of the estate of the
  author, and any agreement entered into by the author as to the
  disposition of such reversionary interest is void.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (1) shall be construed as
  applying to the assignment of the copyright in a collective work or a
  licence to publish a work or part of a work as part of a collective
  work.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1997, c. 24, s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s.
  3\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 14
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 3
- 1997, c. 24, s. 11
:::

**[[14.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.01 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1997,
c. 24, s. 12\]]{.Repealed}

### [Moral Rights]{.HTitleText2} {#h-102861 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Moral rights

- **[[14.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The author of a work has, subject to
  section 28.2, the right to the integrity of the work and, in
  connection with an act mentioned in section 3, the right, where
  reasonable in the circumstances, to be associated with the work as its
  author by name or under a pseudonym and the right to remain anonymous.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No assignment of moral rights

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Moral rights may not be assigned but may be waived in
  whole or in part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No waiver by assignment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An assignment of copyright in a work does not by that
  act alone constitute a waiver of any moral rights.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of waiver

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a waiver of any moral right is made in favour
  of an owner or a licensee of copyright, it may be invoked by any
  person authorized by the owner or licensee to use the work, unless
  there is an indication to the contrary in the waiver.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term

- **[[14.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Moral rights in respect of a work
  subsist for the same term as the copyright in the work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Succession

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The moral rights in respect of a work pass, on the
  death of its author, to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom those rights are specifically
    bequeathed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where there is no specific bequest of those moral
    rights and the author dies testate in respect of the copyright in
    the work, the person to whom that copyright is bequeathed; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where there is no person described in paragraph (a)
    or (b), the person entitled to any other property in respect of
    which the author dies intestate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent succession

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) applies, with such modifications as
  the circumstances require, on the death of any person who holds moral
  rights.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 4
- 1997, c. 24, s. 13
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Copyright in Performers' Performances, Sound Recordings and Communication Signals and Moral Rights in Performers' Performances]{.HTitleText1} {#h-102885 .Part}

### [Performers' Rights]{.HTitleText2} {#h-102886 .Subheading}

#### [Copyright]{.HTitleText3} {#h-102887 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright in performer's performance

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  performer has a copyright in the performer's performance, consisting
  of the sole right to do the following in relation to the performer's
  performance or any substantial part thereof:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if it is not fixed,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to communicate it to the public by
      telecommunication,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to perform it in public, where it is
      communicated to the public by telecommunication otherwise than by
      communication signal, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to fix it in any material form,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if it is fixed,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to reproduce any fixation that was made without
      the performer's authorization,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the performer authorized a fixation, to
      reproduce any reproduction of that fixation, if the reproduction
      being reproduced was made for a purpose other than that for which
      the performer's authorization was given, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} where a fixation was permitted under Part III
      or VIII, to reproduce any reproduction of that fixation, if the
      reproduction being reproduced was made for a purpose other than
      one permitted under Part III or VIII, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to rent out a sound recording of it,

  and to authorize any such acts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright in performer's performance

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2.1) and (2.2), a
  performer's copyright in the performer's performance consists of the
  sole right to do the following acts in relation to the performer's
  performance or any substantial part of it and to authorize any of
  those acts:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if it is not fixed,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to communicate it to the public by
      telecommunication,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to perform it in public, if it is communicated
      to the public by telecommunication otherwise than by communication
      signal, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to fix it in any material form;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if it is fixed in a sound recording, to reproduce
    that fixation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to rent out a sound recording of it;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to make a sound recording of it available to the
    public by telecommunication in a way that allows a member of the
    public to have access to the sound recording from a place and at a
    time individually chosen by that member of the public and to
    communicate the sound recording to the public by telecommunication
    in that way; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if it is fixed in a sound recording that is in the
    form of a tangible object, to sell or otherwise transfer ownership
    of the tangible object, as long as that ownership has never
    previously been transferred in or outside Canada with the
    authorization of the owner of the copyright in the performer's
    performance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies only if the performer's
  performance

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} takes place in Canada or in a Rome Convention
    country;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is fixed in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a sound recording whose maker, at the time of the
      first fixation,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} if a natural person, was a Canadian citizen or
        permanent resident within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the
        [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5), or a
        citizen or permanent resident of a Rome Convention country, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} if a corporation, had its headquarters in
        Canada or in a Rome Convention country, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a sound recording whose first publication in
      such a quantity as to satisfy the reasonable demands of the public
      occurred in Canada or in a Rome Convention country; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is transmitted at the time of the performer's
    performance by a communication signal broadcast from Canada or a
    Rome Convention country by a broadcaster that has its headquarters
    in the country of broadcast.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for copyright

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1.1) applies if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the performer's performance takes place in Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the performer's performance is fixed in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a sound recording whose maker, at the time of its
      first fixation,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} was a Canadian citizen or permanent resident as
        defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee
        Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5), in the case of a natural
        person, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} had its headquarters in Canada, in the case of
        a corporation, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a sound recording whose first publication in a
      quantity sufficient to satisfy the reasonable demands of the
      public occurred in Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the performer's performance is transmitted at the
    time of its performance by a communication signal broadcast from
    Canada by a broadcaster that has its headquarters in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for copyright

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1.1) also applies if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the performer's performance takes place in a WPPT
    country;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the performer's performance is fixed in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a sound recording whose maker, at the time of its
      first fixation,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} was a citizen or permanent resident of a WPPT
        country, in the case of a natural person, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} had its headquarters in a WPPT country, in the
        case of a corporation, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a sound recording whose first publication in a
      quantity sufficient to satisfy the reasonable demands of the
      public occurred in a WPPT country; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the performer's performance is transmitted at the
    time of its performance by a communication signal broadcast from a
    WPPT country by a broadcaster that has its headquarters in that
    country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The first publication is deemed to have occurred in a
  country referred to in paragraph (2)(b) notwithstanding that it in
  fact occurred previously elsewhere, if the interval between those two
  publications does not exceed thirty days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The first publication of a sound recording is deemed
  to have occurred in a WPPT country, despite an earlier publication
  elsewhere, if the interval between the publication in that WPPT
  country and the earlier publication does not exceed 30 days.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 15
- 1993, c. 44, s. 61
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
- 2001, c. 27, s. 235
- 2012, c. 20, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contractual arrangements

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in section 15
prevents the performer from entering into a contract governing the use
of the performer's performance for the purpose of broadcasting, fixation
or retransmission.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 16
- 1994, c. 47, s. 59
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cinematographic works

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  the performer authorizes the embodiment of the performer's performance
  in a cinematographic work, the performer may no longer exercise, in
  relation to the performance where embodied in that cinematographic
  work, the copyright referred to in subsection 15(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to remuneration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where there is an agreement governing the embodiment
  referred to in subsection (1) and that agreement provides for a right
  to remuneration for the reproduction, performance in public or
  communication to the public by telecommunication of the
  cinematographic work, the performer may enforce that right against

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the other party to the agreement or, if that party
    assigns the agreement, the assignee, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other person who

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} owns the copyright in the cinematographic work
      governing the reproduction of the cinematographic work, its
      performance in public or its communication to the public by
      telecommunication, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} reproduces the cinematographic work, performs it
      in public or communicates it to the public by telecommunication,

  and persons referred to in paragraphs (a) and (b) are jointly and
  severally liable to the performer in respect of the remuneration
  relating to that copyright.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsection (2)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) applies only if the performer's
  performance is embodied in a prescribed cinematographic work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If so requested by a country that is a party to the
  Canada--United States--Mexico Agreement, the Minister may, by a
  statement published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), grant the benefits conferred by
  this section, subject to any terms and conditions specified in the
  statement, to performers who are nationals of that country or another
  country that is a party to that Agreement or are Canadian citizens or
  permanent residents within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the
  [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) and whose
  performer's performances are embodied in works other than the
  prescribed cinematographic works referred to in subsection (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 17
- 1994, c. 47, s. 59
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
- 2001, c. 27, s. 236
- [2020, c. 1, s. 27](#1237538-1253473){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237538-1253473.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 17"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Moral Rights]{.HTitleText3} {#h-102964 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Moral rights

- **[[17.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  the cases referred to in subsections 15(2.1) and (2.2), a performer of
  a live aural performance or a performance fixed in a sound recording
  has, subject to subsection 28.2(1), the right to the integrity of the
  performance, and --- in connection with an act mentioned in subsection
  15(1.1) or one for which the performer has a right to remuneration
  under section 19 --- the right, if it is reasonable in the
  circumstances, to be associated with the performance as its performer
  by name or under a pseudonym and the right to remain anonymous.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No assignment of moral rights

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Moral rights may not be assigned but may be waived in
  whole or in part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No waiver by assignment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An assignment of copyright in a performer's
  performance does not by itself constitute a waiver of any moral
  rights.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of waiver

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a waiver of any moral right is made in favour of
  an owner or a licensee of a copyright, it may be invoked by any person
  authorized by the owner or licensee to use the performer's
  performance, unless there is an indication to the contrary in the
  waiver.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 10
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application and term

- **[[17.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 17.1(1) applies only in
  respect of a performer's performance that occurs after the coming into
  force of that subsection. The moral rights subsist for the same term
  as the copyright in that performer's performance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Succession

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The moral rights in respect of a performer's
  performance pass, on the performer's death, to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom those rights are specifically
    bequeathed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if there is not a specific bequest of those moral
    rights and the performer dies testate in respect of the copyright in
    the performer's performance, the person to whom that copyright is
    bequeathed; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if there is not a person as described in
    paragraph (a) or (b), the person entitled to any other property in
    respect of which the performer dies intestate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent succession

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) applies, with any modifications that
  the circumstances require, on the death of any person who holds moral
  rights.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 10
:::

### [Rights of Sound Recording Makers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-102988 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright in sound recordings

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the maker
  of a sound recording has a copyright in the sound recording,
  consisting of the sole right to do the following in relation to the
  sound recording or any substantial part thereof:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to publish it for the first time,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to reproduce it in any material form, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to rent it out,

  and to authorize any such acts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright in sound recordings

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2.1) and (2.2), a sound
  recording maker's copyright in the sound recording also includes the
  sole right to do the following acts in relation to the sound recording
  or any substantial part of it and to authorize any of those acts:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to make it available to the public by
    telecommunication in a way that allows a member of the public to
    have access to it from a place and at a time individually chosen by
    that member of the public and to communicate it to the public by
    telecommunication in that way; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if it is in the form of a tangible object, to sell
    or otherwise transfer ownership of the tangible object, as long as
    that ownership has never previously been transferred in or outside
    Canada with the authorization of the owner of the copyright in the
    sound recording.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for copyright

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the time of the first fixation or, if that first
    fixation was extended over a considerable period, during any
    substantial part of that period, the maker of the sound recording

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} was a Canadian citizen or permanent resident as
      defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee
      Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} was a citizen or permanent resident of a Berne
      Convention country, a Rome Convention country, a WPPT country or a
      country that is a WTO Member, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} had its headquarters in one of those countries,
      in the case of a corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the first publication of the sound recording in a
    quantity sufficient to satisfy the reasonable demands of the public
    occurred in any country referred to in paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for copyright

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1.1) applies if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the time of the first fixation or, if that first
    fixation was extended over a considerable period, during any
    substantial part of that period, the maker of the sound recording

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} was a Canadian citizen or permanent resident as
      defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee
      Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} had its headquarters in Canada, in the case of a
      corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the first publication of the sound recording in a
    quantity sufficient to satisfy the reasonable demands of the public
    occurred in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for copyright

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1.1) also applies if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the time of the first fixation or, if that first
    fixation was extended over a considerable period, during any
    substantial part of that period, the maker of the sound recording

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} was a citizen or permanent resident of a WPPT
      country, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} had its headquarters in a WPPT country, in the
      case of a corporation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the first publication of the sound recording in a
    quantity sufficient to satisfy the reasonable demands of the public
    occurred in a WPPT country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The first publication is deemed to have occurred in a
  country referred to in paragraph (2)(a) notwithstanding that it in
  fact occurred previously elsewhere, if the interval between those two
  publications does not exceed thirty days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The first publication of a sound recording is deemed
  to have occurred in a WPPT country, despite an earlier publication
  elsewhere, if the interval between the publication in that WPPT
  country and the earlier publication does not exceed 30 days.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 18
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 17(F)
- 1994, c. 47, s. 59
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
- 2001, c. 27, s. 237
- 2012, c. 20, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 18"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Provisions Applicable to both Performers and Sound Recording Makers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-103025 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to remuneration --- Canada

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  sound recording has been published, the performer and maker are
  entitled, subject to subsection 20(1), to be paid equitable
  remuneration for its performance in public or its communication to the
  public by telecommunication, except for a communication in the
  circumstances referred to in paragraph 15(1.1)(d) or 18(1.1)(a) and
  any retransmission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to remuneration --- Rome
  Convention country

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If a sound recording has been published, the
  performer and maker are entitled, subject to subsections 20(1.1) and
  (2), to be paid equitable remuneration for its performance in public
  or its communication to the public by telecommunication, except for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a communication in the circumstances referred to in
    paragraph 15(1.1)(d) or 18(1.1)(a), if the person entitled to the
    equitable remuneration is entitled to the right referred to in those
    paragraphs for that communication; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any retransmission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to remuneration --- WPPT country

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If a sound recording has been published, the
  performer and maker are entitled, subject to subsections 20(1.2) and
  (2.1), to be paid equitable remuneration for its performance in public
  or its communication to the public by telecommunication, except for a
  communication in the circumstances referred to in paragraph 15(1.1)(d)
  or 18(1.1)(a) and any retransmission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Royalties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of providing the remuneration
  mentioned in this section, a person who performs a published sound
  recording in public or communicates it to the public by
  telecommunication is liable to pay royalties

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a sound recording of a musical work,
    to the collective society authorized under Part VII.1 to collect
    them; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a sound recording of a literary work
    or dramatic work, to either the maker of the sound recording or the
    performer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division of royalties

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The royalties, once paid pursuant to paragraph (2)(a)
  or (b), shall be divided so that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the performer or performers receive in aggregate
    fifty per cent; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maker or makers receive in aggregate fifty per
    cent.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 19
- 1994, c. 47, s. 59
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
- 2012, c. 20, s. 12
- [2018, c. 27, s. 281](#1039396-1121270){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1039396-1121270.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19-20140813.html "Link to previous version of section 19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed publication --- Canada

**[[19.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.1 .sectionLabel}** Despite subsection
2.2(1), a sound recording that has been made available to the public by
telecommunication in a way that allows a member of the public to access
it from a place and at a time individually chosen by that member of the
public, or that has been communicated to the public by telecommunication
in that way, is deemed to have been published for the purposes of
subsection 19(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 13
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed publication --- WPPT country

**[[19.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.2 .sectionLabel}** Despite subsection
2.2(1), a sound recording that has been made available to the public by
telecommunication in a way that allows a member of the public to access
it from a place and at a time individually chosen by that member of the
public, or that has been communicated to the public by telecommunication
in that way, is deemed to have been published for the purposes of
subsection 19(1.2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 14
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- Canada

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  right to remuneration conferred by subsection 19(1) applies only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the maker was, at the date of the first fixation, a
    Canadian citizen or permanent resident within the meaning of
    subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
    Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) or, if a corporation, had its headquarters in
    Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all the fixations done for the sound recording
    occurred in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- Rome Convention country

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The right to remuneration conferred by subsection
  19(1.1) applies only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the maker was, at the date of the first fixation, a
    citizen or permanent resident of a Rome Convention country or, if a
    corporation, had its headquarters in a Rome Convention country; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all the fixations done for the sound recording
    occurred in a Rome Convention country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- WPPT country

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The right to remuneration conferred by subsection
  19(1.2) applies only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the maker was, at the date of the first fixation, a
    citizen or permanent resident of a WPPT country or, if a
    corporation, had its headquarters in a WPPT country; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all the fixations done for the sound recording
    occurred in a WPPT country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- Rome Convention country

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1.1), if the Minister is of the
  opinion that a Rome Convention country does not grant a right to
  remuneration, similar in scope and duration to that provided by
  subsection 19(1.1), for the performance in public or the communication
  to the public of a sound recording whose maker, at the date of its
  first fixation, was a Canadian citizen or permanent resident within
  the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee
  Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) or, if a corporation, had its
  headquarters in Canada, the Minister may, by a statement published in
  the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), limit the scope and
  duration of the protection for sound recordings whose first fixation
  is done by a maker who is a citizen or permanent resident of that
  country or, if a corporation, has its headquarters in that country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- WPPT country

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1.2), if the Minister is of the
  opinion that a WPPT country does not grant a right to remuneration,
  similar in scope and duration to that provided by subsection 19(1.2),
  for the performance in public or the communication to the public of a
  sound recording whose maker, at the date of its first fixation, was a
  Canadian citizen or permanent resident within the meaning of
  subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
  Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) or, if a corporation, had its headquarters in
  Canada, the Minister may, by a statement published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), limit the scope and duration of
  the protection for sound recordings whose first fixation is done by a
  maker who is a citizen or permanent resident of that country or, if a
  corporation, has its headquarters in that country.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2020, c. 1, s. 28\]]{.Repealed}

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2020, c. 1, s. 28\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 20
- 1994, c. 47, s. 59
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
- 2001, c. 27, s. 238
- 2012, c. 20, s. 15

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237542-1253476.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Rights of Broadcasters]{.HTitleText2} {#h-103078 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright in communication signals

- **[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  broadcaster has a copyright in the communication signals that it
  broadcasts, consisting of the sole right to do the following in
  relation to the communication signal or any substantial part thereof:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to fix it,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to reproduce any fixation of it that was made
    without the broadcaster's consent,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to authorize another broadcaster to retransmit it
    to the public simultaneously with its broadcast, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a television communication signal,
    to perform it in a place open to the public on payment of an
    entrance fee,

  and to authorize any act described in paragraph (a), (b) or (d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for copyright

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies only if the broadcaster

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the time of the broadcast, had its headquarters
    in Canada, in a country that is a WTO Member or in a Rome Convention
    country; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} broadcasts the communication signal from that
    country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (2), if the Minister is of
  the opinion that a Rome Convention country or a country that is a WTO
  Member does not grant the right mentioned in paragraph (1)(d), the
  Minister may, by a statement published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), declare that broadcasters that
  have their headquarters in that country are not entitled to that
  right.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 21
- 1994, c. 47, s. 59
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
:::

### [Reciprocity]{.HTitleText2} {#h-103095 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reciprocity

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  Minister is of the opinion that a country other than a Rome Convention
  country or a WPPT country grants or has undertaken to grant

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to performers and to makers of sound recordings, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to broadcasters

  that are Canadian citizens or permanent residents within the meaning
  of subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
  Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) or, if corporations, have their headquarters in
  Canada, as the case may be, whether by treaty, convention, agreement
  or law, benefits substantially equivalent to those conferred by this
  Part, the Minister may, by a statement published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/),

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} grant the benefits conferred by this Part

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to performers and to makers of sound recordings,
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to broadcasters

    as the case may be, that are citizens, subjects or permanent
    residents of or, if corporations, have their headquarters in that
    country, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} declare that that country shall, as regards those
    benefits, be treated as if it were a country to which this Part
    extends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reciprocity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is of the opinion that a country
  other than a Rome Convention country or a WPPT country neither grants
  nor has undertaken to grant

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to performers, and to makers of sound recordings,
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to broadcasters

  that are Canadian citizens or permanent residents within the meaning
  of subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
  Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) or, if corporations, have their headquarters in
  Canada, as the case may be, whether by treaty, convention, agreement
  or law, benefits substantially equivalent to those conferred by this
  Part, the Minister may, by a statement published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/),

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} grant the benefits conferred by this Part to
    performers, makers of sound recordings or broadcasters that are
    citizens, subjects or permanent residents of or, if corporations,
    have their headquarters in that country, as the case may be, to the
    extent that that country grants that those benefits to performers,
    makers of sound recordings or broadcasters that are Canadian
    citizens or permanent residents within the meaning of subsection
    2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
    Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5) or, if corporations, have their headquarters
    in Canada, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} declare that that country shall, as regards those
    benefits, be treated as if it were a country to which this Part
    extends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any provision of this Act that the Minister specifies
  in a statement referred to in subsection (1) or (2)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applies in respect of performers, makers of sound
    recordings or broadcasters covered by that statement, as if they
    were citizens of or, if corporations, had their headquarters in
    Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} applies in respect of a country covered by that
    statement, as if that country were Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Act

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any exceptions that the Minister may
  specify in a statement referred to in subsection (1) or (2), the other
  provisions of this Act also apply in the way described in subsection
  (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 22
- 1994, c. 47, s. 59
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
- 2001, c. 27, s. 239
- 2012, c. 20, s. 16
:::

:::

### [Term of Rights]{.HTitleText2} {#h-103122 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of copyright --- performer's
performance

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act, copyright in a
  performer's performance subsists until the end of 50 years after the
  end of the calendar year in which the performance occurs. However,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the performance is fixed in a sound recording
    before the copyright expires, the copyright continues until the end
    of 70 years after the end of the calendar year in which the first
    fixation of the performance in a sound recording occurs; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if a sound recording in which the performance is
    fixed is published before the copyright expires, the copyright
    continues until the earlier of the end of 75 years after the end of
    the calendar year in which the first such publication occurs and the
    end of 100 years after the end of the calendar year in which the
    first fixation of the performance in a sound recording occurs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of copyright --- sound recording

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act, copyright in a sound recording
  subsists until the end of 70 years after the end of the calendar year
  in which the first fixation of the sound recording occurs. However, if
  the sound recording is published before the copyright expires, the
  copyright continues until the earlier of the end of 75 years after the
  end of the calendar year in which the first publication of the sound
  recording occurs and the end of 100 years after the end of the
  calendar year in which that first fixation occurs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of copyright --- communication
  signal

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act, copyright in a communication
  signal subsists until the end of 50 years after the end of the
  calendar year in which the communication signal is broadcast.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of right to remuneration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The rights to remuneration conferred on performers
  and makers by section 19 have the same terms, respectively, as those
  provided by subsections (1) and (1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections (1) to (2)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) to (2) apply whether the fixation,
  performance or broadcast occurred before or after the coming into
  force of this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Berne Convention countries, Rome
  Convention countries, WTO Members

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the performer's performance, sound recording or
  communication signal meets the requirements set out in section 15, 18
  or 21, as the case may be, a country that becomes a Berne Convention
  country, a Rome Convention country or a WTO Member after the date of
  the fixation, performance or broadcast is, as of becoming a Berne
  Convention country, Rome Convention country or WTO Member, as the case
  may be, deemed to have been such at the date of the fixation,
  performance or broadcast.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where term of protection expired

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not confer any protection in
  Canada where the term of protection in the country referred to in that
  subsection had expired before that country became a Berne Convention
  country, Rome Convention country or WTO Member, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 23
- 1994, c. 47, s. 59
- 1997, c. 24, s. 14
- 2012, c. 20, s. 17
- 2015, c. 36, s. 81
- [2020, c. 1, s. 29](#1237545-1253481){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237545-1253481.html"
  role="button"}
:::
