[Previous
Version](section-30.5-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 30.5"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Computer Programs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103634 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permitted acts

**[[30.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30.6 .sectionLabel}** It is not an
infringement of copyright in a computer program for a person who owns a
copy of the computer program that is authorized by the owner of the
copyright, or has a licence to use a copy of the computer program, to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} reproduce the copy by adapting, modifying or
  converting it, or translating it into another computer language, if
  the person proves that the reproduced copy

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is essential for the compatibility of the computer
    program with a particular computer,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is solely for the person's own use, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} was destroyed immediately after the person ceased
    to be the owner of the copy of the computer program or to have a
    licence to use it; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} reproduce for backup purposes the copy or a
  reproduced copy referred to in paragraph (a) if the person proves that
  the reproduction for backup purposes was destroyed immediately after
  the person ceased to be the owner of the copy of the computer program
  or to have a licence to use it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 18
- 2012, c. 20, s. 31
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30.6-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 30.6"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interoperability of computer programs

- **[[30.61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30.61
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It is not an infringement of
  copyright in a computer program for a person who owns a copy of the
  computer program that is authorized by the owner of the copyright, or
  has a licence to use a copy of the computer program, to reproduce the
  copy if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they reproduce the copy for the sole purpose of
    obtaining information that would allow the person to make the
    program and another computer program interoperable; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they do not use or disclose that information,
    except as necessary to make the program and another computer program
    interoperable or to assess that interoperability.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case where that information is used or
  disclosed as necessary to make another computer program interoperable
  with the program, subsection (1) applies even if the other computer
  program incorporates the information and is then sold, rented or
  otherwise distributed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 31
:::

#### [Encryption Research]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103653 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Encryption research

- **[[30.62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30.62
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  it is not an infringement of copyright for a person to reproduce a
  work or other subject-matter for the purposes of encryption research
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it would not be practical to carry out the research
    without making the copy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person has lawfully obtained the work or other
    subject-matter; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person has informed the owner of the copyright
    in the work or other subject-matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the person uses or
  discloses information obtained through the research to commit an act
  that is an offence under the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation  --- computer program

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies with respect to a computer
  program only if, in the event that the research reveals a
  vulnerability or a security flaw in the program and the person intends
  to make the vulnerability or security flaw public, the person gives
  adequate notice of the vulnerability or security flaw and of their
  intention to the owner of copyright in the program. However, the
  person need not give that adequate notice if, in the circumstances,
  the public interest in having the vulnerability or security flaw made
  public without adequate notice outweighs the owner's interest in
  receiving that notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 31
:::

#### [Security]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103666 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security

- **[[30.63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30.63
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  it is not an infringement of copyright for a person to reproduce a
  work or other subject-matter for the sole purpose, with the consent of
  the owner or administrator of a computer, computer system or computer
  network, of assessing the vulnerability of the computer, system or
  network or of correcting any security flaws.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the person uses or
  discloses information obtained through the assessment or correction to
  commit an act that is an offence under the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation  --- computer program

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies with respect to a computer
  program only if, in the event that the assessment or correction
  reveals a vulnerability or a security flaw in the program and the
  person intends to make the vulnerability or security flaw public, the
  person gives adequate notice of the vulnerability or security flaw and
  of their intention to the owner of copyright in the program. However,
  the person need not give that adequate notice if, in the
  circumstances, the public interest in having the vulnerability or
  security flaw made public without adequate notice outweighs the
  owner's interest in receiving that notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 31
:::

#### [Incidental Inclusion]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103676 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incidental use

**[[30.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30.7 .sectionLabel}** It is not an
infringement of copyright to incidentally and not deliberately

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} include a work or other subject-matter in another
  work or other subject-matter; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} do any act in relation to a work or other
  subject-matter that is incidentally and not deliberately included in
  another work or other subject-matter.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 18
:::

#### [Temporary Reproductions for Technological Processes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103683 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary reproductions

**[[30.71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30.71 .sectionLabel}** It is not an
infringement of copyright to make a reproduction of a work or other
subject-matter if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reproduction forms an essential part of a
  technological process;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reproduction's only purpose is to facilitate a
  use that is not an infringement of copyright; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the reproduction exists only for the duration of the
  technological process.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 32
:::

#### [Ephemeral Recordings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103691 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ephemeral recordings

- **[[30.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30.8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It
  is not an infringement of copyright for a programming undertaking to
  fix or reproduce in accordance with this section a performer's
  performance or work, other than a cinematographic work, that is
  performed live or a sound recording that is performed at the same time
  as the performer's performance or work, if the undertaking

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is authorized to communicate the performer's
    performance, work or sound recording to the public by
    telecommunication;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} makes the fixation or the reproduction itself, for
    its own broadcasts;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not synchronize the fixation or reproduction
    with all or part of another recording, performer's performance or
    work; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} does not cause the fixation or reproduction to be
    used in an advertisement intended to sell or promote, as the case
    may be, a product, service, cause or institution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record keeping

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The programming undertaking must record the dates of
  the making and destruction of all fixations and reproductions and any
  other prescribed information about the fixation or reproduction, and
  keep the record current.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of access by copyright owners

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The programming undertaking must make the record
  referred to in subsection (2) available to owners of copyright in the
  works, sound recordings or performer's performances, or their
  representatives, within twenty-four hours after receiving a request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The programming undertaking must destroy the fixation
  or reproduction within thirty days after making it, unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the copyright owner authorizes its retention; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is deposited in an archive, in accordance with
    subsection (6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Royalties

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the copyright owner authorizes the fixation or
  reproduction to be retained after the thirty days, the programming
  undertaking must pay any applicable royalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Archive

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where the programming undertaking considers a
  fixation or reproduction to be of an exceptional documentary
  character, the undertaking may, with the consent of an official
  archive, deposit it in the official archive and must notify the
  copyright owner, within thirty days, of the deposit of the fixation or
  reproduction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[official
  archive]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In subsection (6), [[official
  archive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Library and Archives of Canada
  or any archive established under the law of a province for the
  preservation of the official archives of the province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply where a licence is
  available from a collective society to make the fixation or
  reproduction of the performer's performance, work or sound recording.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Telecommunications by networks

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A broadcasting undertaking, as defined in the
  [Broadcasting Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01), may make a single reproduction
  of a fixation or reproduction made by a programming undertaking and
  communicate it to the public by telecommunication, within the period
  referred to in subsection (4), if the broadcasting undertaking meets
  the conditions set out in subsection (1) and is part of a prescribed
  network that includes the programming undertaking.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitations

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The reproduction and communication to the public by
  telecommunication must be made

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with subsections (2) to (6); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within thirty days after the day on which the
    programming undertaking made the fixation or reproduction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[programming
  undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[programming
  undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a [[programming
    undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection 2(1)
    of the [Broadcasting Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01), that is carried on
    lawfully under that Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a programming undertaking described in
    paragraph (a) that originates programs within a
    [[network]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection 2(1) of
    the [Broadcasting Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a [[distribution
    undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection 2(1)
    of the [Broadcasting Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01), that is carried on
    lawfully under that Act, in respect of the programs that it
    originates.

  For greater certainty, it does not include an [[online
  undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection 2(1) of
  the [Broadcasting Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 18
- 2004, c. 11, s. 26
- 2012, c. 20, s. 33
- [2023, c. 8, s. 38](#1396703-1396882){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1396703-1396882.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30.8-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 30.8"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ephemeral recordings --- broadcasting
undertaking

- **[[30.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30.9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It
  is not an infringement of copyright for a broadcasting undertaking to
  reproduce in accordance with this section a sound recording, or a
  performer's performance or work that is embodied in a sound recording,
  solely for the purpose of their broadcasting, if the undertaking

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} owns the copy of the sound recording, performer's
    performance or work and that copy is authorized by the owner of the
    copyright, or has a licence to use the copy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is authorized to communicate the sound recording,
    performer's performance or work to the public by telecommunication;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} makes the reproduction itself, for its own
    broadcasts;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} does not synchronize the reproduction with all or
    part of another recording, performer's performance or work; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} does not cause the reproduction to be used in an
    advertisement intended to sell or promote, as the case may be, a
    product, service, cause or institution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record keeping

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The broadcasting undertaking must record the dates of
  the making and destruction of all reproductions and any other
  prescribed information about the reproduction, and keep the record
  current.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of access by copyright owners

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The broadcasting undertaking must make the record
  referred to in subsection (2) available to owners of copyright in the
  sound recordings, performer's performances or works, or their
  representatives, within twenty-four hours after receiving a request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The broadcasting undertaking must destroy the
  reproduction when it no longer possesses the sound recording, or
  performer's performance or work embodied in the sound recording, or
  its licence to use the sound recording, performer's performance or
  work expires, or at the latest within 30 days after making the
  reproduction, unless the copyright owner authorizes the reproduction
  to be retained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Royalty

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the copyright owner authorizes the reproduction to
  be retained, the broadcasting undertaking must pay any applicable
  royalty.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 20, s. 34\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[broadcasting
  undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[broadcasting
  undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a [[broadcasting
  undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection 2(1) of
  the [Broadcasting Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01), that holds a broadcasting
  licence issued by the Canadian Radio-television and Telecommunications
  Commission under that Act. For greater certainty, it does not include
  an [[online undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in that
  subsection 2(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 18
- 2012, c. 20, s. 34
- [2023, c. 8, s. 39](#1396707-1396885){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1396707-1396885.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-30.9-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 30.9"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Retransmission]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103750 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section,

  [[new media retransmitter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[new media retransmitter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person
      whose retransmission would be lawful under the [Broadcasting
      Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01) --- as that Act read immediately before the
      day on which section 31.1 of that Act comes into force --- only by
      reason of the Exemption order for digital media broadcasting
      undertakings, issued by the Canadian Radio-television and
      Telecommunications Commission as the appendix to Broadcasting
      Order CRTC 2012-409, as it read immediately before that
      day; ([retransmetteur de nouveaux médias]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[retransmitter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[retransmitter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who performs
      a function comparable to that of a cable retransmission system,
      but does not include a new media
      retransmitter; ([retransmetteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[signal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[signal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a signal that carries a
      literary, dramatic, musical or artistic work and is transmitted
      for free reception by the public by a terrestrial radio or
      terrestrial television station. ([signal]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retransmission of local and distant
  signals

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} It is not an infringement of copyright for a
  retransmitter to communicate to the public by telecommunication any
  literary, dramatic, musical or artistic work if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the communication is a retransmission of a local or
    distant signal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the retransmission is lawful under the
    [Broadcasting Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the signal is retransmitted simultaneously and
    without alteration, except as otherwise required or permitted by or
    under the laws of Canada;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the retransmission of a distant
    signal, the retransmitter has paid any royalties, and complied with
    any terms and conditions, fixed under this Act; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the retransmitter complies with the applicable
    conditions, if any, referred to in paragraph (3)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining "local signal" and "distant signal" for
    the purposes of subsection (2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing conditions for the purposes of
    paragraph (2)(e), and specifying whether any such condition applies
    to all retransmitters or only to a class of retransmitter.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 31
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 7
- 1988, c. 65, s. 63
- 1997, c. 24, ss. 16, 52(F)
- 2002, c. 26, s. 2
- [2023, c. 8, s. 40](#1396711-1396887){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1396711-1396887.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-31-20030321.html "Link to previous version of section 31"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Network Services]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103770 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Network services

- **[[31.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who, in providing services related to the operation of the
  Internet or another digital network, provides any means for the
  telecommunication or the reproduction of a work or other
  subject-matter through the Internet or that other network does not,
  solely by reason of providing those means, infringe copyright in that
  work or other subject-matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incidental acts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a person referred to in
  subsection (1) who caches the work or other subject-matter, or does
  any similar act in relation to it, to make the telecommunication more
  efficient does not, by virtue of that act alone, infringe copyright in
  the work or other subject-matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply unless the person, in
  respect of the work or other subject-matter,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} does not modify it, other than for technical
    reasons;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensures that any directions related to its caching
    or the doing of any similar act, as the case may be, that are
    specified in a manner consistent with industry practice by whoever
    made it available for telecommunication through the Internet or
    another digital network, and that lend themselves to automated
    reading and execution, are read and executed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not interfere with the use of technology that
    is lawful and consistent with industry practice in order to obtain
    data on the use of the work or other subject-matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hosting

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), a person who, for the
  purpose of allowing the telecommunication of a work or other
  subject-matter through the Internet or another digital network,
  provides digital memory in which another person stores the work or
  other subject-matter does not, by virtue of that act alone, infringe
  copyright in the work or other subject-matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition for application

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not apply in respect of a work or
  other subject-matter if the person providing the digital memory knows
  of a decision of a court of competent jurisdiction to the effect that
  the person who has stored the work or other subject-matter in the
  digital memory infringes copyright by making the copy of the work or
  other subject-matter that is stored or by the way in which he or she
  uses the work or other subject-matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1), (2) and (4) do not apply in relation
  to an act that constitutes an infringement of copyright under
  subsection 27(2.3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 35
:::

#### [Persons with Perceptual Disabilities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103789 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reproduction in alternate format

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It is
  not an infringement of copyright for a person with a perceptual
  disability, for a person acting at the request of such a person or for
  a non-profit organization acting for the benefit of such a person to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reproduce a literary, musical, artistic or dramatic
    work, other than a cinematographic work, in a format specially
    designed for persons with a perceptual disability;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} fix a performer's performance of a literary,
    musical, artistic or dramatic work, other than a cinematographic
    work, in a format specially designed for persons with a perceptual
    disability;

  - [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} reproduce a sound recording, or a fixation of a
    performer's performance referred to in paragraph (a.1), in a format
    specially designed for persons with a perceptual disability;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} translate, adapt or reproduce in sign language a
    literary or dramatic work, other than a cinematographic work, in a
    format specially designed for persons with a perceptual disability;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} provide a person with a perceptual disability
    with, or provide such a person with access to, a work or other
    subject-matter to which any of paragraphs (a) to (b) applies, in a
    format specially designed for persons with a perceptual disability,
    and do any other act that is necessary for that purpose; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} perform in public a literary or dramatic work,
    other than a cinematographic work, in sign language, either live or
    in a format specially designed for persons with a perceptual
    disability.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the work or other
  subject-matter is commercially available, within the meaning of
  paragraph (a) of the definition [[commercially
  available]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 2, in a format specially
  designed to meet the needs of the person with a perceptual disability
  referred to in that subsection.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2016, c. 4, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 32
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 7
- 1997, c. 24, s. 19
- 2012, c. 20, s. 36
- 2016, c. 4, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 32"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Print disability  ---  outside Canada

- **[[32.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, it is not
  an infringement of copyright for a non-profit organization acting for
  the benefit of persons with a print disability to do any of the
  following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of doing any of the acts set out in
    paragraph (b),

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} reproduce a literary, musical, artistic or
      dramatic work, other than a cinematographic work, in a format
      specially designed for persons with a print disability,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} fix a performer's performance of a literary,
      musical, artistic or dramatic work, other than a cinematographic
      work, in a format specially designed for persons with a print
      disability, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} reproduce a sound recording, or a fixation of a
      performer's performance referred to in subparagraph (ii), in a
      format specially designed for persons with a print disability;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide either of the following with, or provide
    either of the following with access to, a work or other
    subject-matter to which any of subparagraphs (a)(i) to (iii)
    applies, in a format specially designed for persons with a print
    disability, and do any other act that is necessary for that purpose:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a non-profit organization, in a country other
      than Canada, acting for the benefit of persons with a print
      disability in that country, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a person with a print disability, in a country
      other than Canada, who has made a request to be provided with, or
      provided with access to, the work or other subject-matter through
      a non-profit organization acting for the benefit of persons with a
      print disability in that country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Available in other country

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(b) does not apply if the work or other
  subject-matter, in the format specially designed for persons with a
  print disability, is available in the other country within a
  reasonable time and for a reasonable price and may be located in that
  country with reasonable effort.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Marrakesh Treaty country

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An injunction is the only remedy that the owner of
  the copyright in the work or other subject-matter has against a
  non-profit organization relying on the exception set out in paragraph
  (1)(b) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the other country referred to in that paragraph is
    a Marrakesh Treaty country; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the non-profit organization infringes copyright by
    reason only that the work or other subject-matter, in the format
    described in subsection (2), is available, and may be located, as
    described in that subsection.

  The owner of the copyright bears the burden of demonstrating that the
  work or other subject-matter, in the format described in subsection
  (2), is available, and may be located, as described in that
  subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not Marrakesh Treaty country

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} An injunction is the only remedy that the owner of
  the copyright in the work or other subject-matter has against a
  non-profit organization relying on the exception set out in paragraph
  (1)(b) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the other country referred to in that paragraph is
    not a Marrakesh Treaty country;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the non-profit organization infringes copyright by
    reason only that the work or other subject-matter, in the format
    described in subsection (2), is available, and may be located, as
    described in that subsection; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the non-profit organization demonstrates that it
    had reasonable grounds to believe that the work or other
    subject-matter, in the format described in subsection (2), was not
    available, and could not be located, as described in that
    subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Royalty

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A non-profit organization relying on the exception
  set out in subsection (1) shall pay, in accordance with the
  regulations, any royalty established under the regulations to the
  copyright owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If copyright owner cannot be located

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the organization cannot locate the copyright
  owner, despite making reasonable efforts to do so, the organization
  shall pay, in accordance with the regulations, any royalty established
  under the regulations to a collective society.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A non-profit organization relying on the exception
  set out in subsection (1) shall submit reports to an authority, in
  accordance with the regulations, on the organization's activities
  under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} requiring that, before a non-profit organization
    provides, or provides access to, a work or other subject-matter
    under paragraph (1)(b), the organization enter into a contract with
    respect to the use of the work or other subject-matter with, as the
    case may be, the recipient non-profit organization or the non-profit
    organization through which the request was made;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the form and content of such contracts;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting any royalties to be paid under
    subsections (4) and (5);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting to which collective society a royalty is
    payable in relation to works or other subject-matter, or classes of
    works or other subject-matter, for the purposes of subsection (5);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting what constitutes reasonable efforts for
    the purposes of subsection (5); and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting the reports to be made, and the
    authorities to which the reports are to be submitted, under
    subsection (6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in this section.

  [[Marrakesh Treaty country]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Marrakesh Treaty country]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a country
      that is a party to the Marrakesh Treaty to Facilitate Access to
      Published Works for Persons Who Are Blind, Visually Impaired, or
      Otherwise Print Disabled, done at Marrakesh on June 27,
      2013. ([pays partie au Traité de Marrakech]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[print disability]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[print disability]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a disability that
      prevents or inhibits a person from reading a literary, musical,
      artistic or dramatic work in its original format and includes such
      a disability resulting from

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} severe or total impairment of sight or the
        inability to focus or move one's eyes;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the inability to hold or manipulate a book; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an impairment relating to
        comprehension. ([déficience de lecture des
        imprimés]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 37
- 2016, c. 4, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32.01-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 32.01"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[non-profit
organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[32.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32.02 .sectionLabel}** In sections 32 and
32.01, [[non-profit organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a
department, agency or other portion of any order of government,
including a municipal or local government, when it is acting on a
non-profit basis.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2016, c. 4, s. 3
:::

#### [Statutory Obligations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103853 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No infringement

- **[[32.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It
  is not an infringement of copyright for any person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to disclose, pursuant to the [Access to Information
    Act](/eng/acts/A-1), a record within the meaning of that Act, or to
    disclose, pursuant to any like Act of the legislature of a province,
    like material;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to disclose, pursuant to the [Privacy
    Act](/eng/acts/P-21), personal information within the meaning of
    that Act, or to disclose, pursuant to any like Act of the
    legislature of a province, like information;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to make a copy of an object referred to in section
    14 of the [Cultural Property Export and Import Act](/eng/acts/C-51),
    for deposit in an institution pursuant to a direction under that
    section; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to make a fixation or copy of a work or other
    subject-matter in order to comply with the [Broadcasting
    Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01) or any rule, regulation or other instrument
    made under it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in paragraph (1)(a) or (b) authorizes a
  person to whom a record or information is disclosed to do anything
  that, by this Act, only the owner of the copyright in the record,
  personal information or like information, as the case may be, has a
  right to do.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction of fixation or copy

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless the [Broadcasting Act](/eng/acts/B-9.01)
  otherwise provides, a person who makes a fixation or copy under
  paragraph (1)(d) shall destroy it immediately on the expiration of the
  period for which it must be kept pursuant to that Act, rule,
  regulation or other instrument.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 19
:::

#### [Miscellaneous]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103867 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permitted acts

- **[[32.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It
  is not an infringement of copyright

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for an author of an artistic work who is not the
    owner of the copyright in the work to use any mould, cast, sketch,
    plan, model or study made by the author for the purpose of the work,
    if the author does not thereby repeat or imitate the main design of
    the work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for any person to reproduce, in a painting,
    drawing, engraving, photograph or cinematographic work

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an architectural work, provided the copy is not
      in the nature of an architectural drawing or plan, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a sculpture or work of artistic craftsmanship or
      a cast or model of a sculpture or work of artistic craftsmanship,
      that is permanently situated in a public place or building;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for any person to make or publish, for the purposes
    of news reporting or news summary, a report of a lecture given in
    public, unless the report is prohibited by conspicuous written or
    printed notice affixed before and maintained during the lecture at
    or about the main entrance of the building in which the lecture is
    given, and, except while the building is being used for public
    worship, in a position near the lecturer;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for any person to read or recite in public a
    reasonable extract from a published work;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for any person to make or publish, for the purposes
    of news reporting or news summary, a report of an address of a
    political nature given at a public meeting; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} for an individual to use for private or
    non-commercial purposes, or permit the use of for those purposes, a
    photograph or portrait that was commissioned by the individual for
    personal purposes and made for valuable consideration, unless the
    individual and the owner of the copyright in the photograph or
    portrait have agreed otherwise.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further permitted acts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} It is not an infringement of copyright for a person
  to do any of the following acts without motive of gain at any
  agricultural or agricultural-industrial exhibition or fair that
  receives a grant from or is held by its directors under federal,
  provincial or municipal authority:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the live performance in public of a musical work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the performance in public of a sound recording
    embodying a musical work or a performer's performance of a musical
    work; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the performance in public of a communication signal
    carrying

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the live performance in public of a musical work,
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a sound recording embodying a musical work or a
      performer's performance of a musical work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further permitted acts

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No religious organization or institution, educational
  institution and no charitable or fraternal organization shall be held
  liable to pay any compensation for doing any of the following acts in
  furtherance of a religious, educational or charitable object:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the live performance in public of a musical work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the performance in public of a sound recording
    embodying a musical work or a performer's performance of a musical
    work; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the performance in public of a communication signal
    carrying

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the live performance in public of a musical work,
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a sound recording embodying a musical work or a
      performer's performance of a musical work.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 19
- 2012, c. 20, s. 38
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32.2-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 32.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-103895 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No right to equitable remuneration

**[[32.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32.3 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 29 to 32.2, an act that does not infringe copyright does not
give rise to a right to remuneration conferred by section 19.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 19
:::

### [Compensation for Acts Done Before Recognition of Copyright of Performers and Broadcasters]{.HTitleText2} {#h-103900 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain rights and interests protected

- **[[32.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 27, where a
  person has, before the later of January 1, 1996 and the day on which a
  country becomes a WTO member, incurred an expenditure or liability in
  connection with, or in preparation for, the doing of an act that would
  have infringed copyright under section 26 commencing on the later of
  those days, had that country been a WTO member, any right or interest
  of that person that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} arises from or in connection with the doing of that
    act, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is subsisting and valuable on the later of those
    days

  is not prejudiced or diminished by reason only that that country has
  become a WTO member, except as provided by an order of the Board made
  under subsection 78(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), a person's right or
  interest that is protected by that subsection terminates if and when
  the owner of the copyright pays that person such compensation as is
  agreed to between the parties or, failing agreement, as is determined
  by the Board in accordance with section 78.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsections (1) and (2) affects any right
  of a performer available in law or equity.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain rights and interests protected

- **[[32.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 27, where a
  person has, before the later of the coming into force of Part II and
  the day on which a country becomes a Rome Convention country, incurred
  an expenditure or liability in connection with, or in preparation for,
  the doing of an act that would have infringed copyright under section
  15 or 21 commencing on the later of those days, had Part II been in
  force or had that country been a Rome Convention country, any right or
  interest of that person that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} arises from or in connection with the doing of that
    act, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is subsisting and valuable on the later of those
    days

  is not prejudiced or diminished by reason only that Part II has come
  into force or that the country has become a Rome Convention country,
  except as provided by an order of the Board made under subsection
  78(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), a person's right or
  interest that is protected by that subsection terminates if and when
  the owner of the copyright pays that person such compensation as is
  agreed to between the parties or, failing agreement, as is determined
  by the Board in accordance with section 78.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsections (1) and (2) affects any right
  of a performer available in law or equity.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain rights and interests protected

**[[32.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32.6 .sectionLabel}** Despite sections 27,
28.1 and 28.2, if a person has, before the day on which subsection
15(1.1), 17.1(1) or 18(1.1) applies in respect of a particular
performers' performance or sound recording, incurred an expenditure or a
liability in connection with, or in preparation for, the doing of an act
that would, if done after that day, have infringed rights under that
subsection, any right or interest of that person that arises from, or in
connection with, the doing of that act and that is subsisting and
valuable on that day is not, for two years after the day on which this
section comes into force, prejudiced or diminished by reason only of the
subsequent application of that subsection in respect of the performers'
performance or sound recording.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 39
:::

### [Compensation for Acts Done Before Recognition of Copyright or Moral Rights]{.HTitleText2} {#h-103929 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain rights and interests protected

- **[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections 27(1), (2)
  and (4) and sections 27.1, 28.1 and 28.2, if a person has, before the
  later of January 1, 1996 and the day on which a country becomes a
  treaty country other than a WCT country, incurred an expenditure or
  liability in connection with, or in preparation for, the doing of an
  act that, if that country had been such a treaty country, would have
  infringed copyright in a work or moral rights in respect of a work,
  any right or interest of that person that arises from, or in
  connection with, the doing of that act and that is subsisting and
  valuable on the later of those days is not, except as provided by an
  order of the Board made under subsection 78(3), prejudiced or
  diminished by reason only of that country having become such a treaty
  country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), a person's right or
  interest that is protected by that subsection terminates, as against
  the copyright owner or author, if and when that copyright owner or the
  author, as the case may be, pays that person such compensation as is
  agreed to between the parties or, failing agreement, as is determined
  by the Board in accordance with section 78.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 33
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 7
- 1997, c. 24, s. 19
- 2012, c. 20, s. 40
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-33-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 33"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain rights and interests protected

- **[[33.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections 27(1), (2)
  and (4) and sections 27.1, 28.1 and 28.2, if a person has, before the
  later of the day on which this section comes into force and the day on
  which a country that is a treaty country but not a WCT country becomes
  a WCT country, incurred an expenditure or liability in connection
  with, or in preparation for, the doing of an act that, if that country
  had been a WCT country, would have infringed a right under paragraph
  3(1)(j), any right or interest of that person that arises from, or in
  connection with, the doing of that act and that is subsisting and
  valuable on the later of those days is not, except as provided by an
  order of the Board made under subsection 78(3), prejudiced or
  diminished by reason only of that country having become a WCT country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a person's right or interest
  that is protected by that subsection terminates as against the
  copyright owner if and when the owner pays the person any compensation
  that is agreed to between the parties or, failing agreement, that is
  determined by the Board in accordance with section 78.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 41
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain rights and interests protected

- **[[33.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections 27(1), (2)
  and (4) and sections 27.1, 28.1 and 28.2, if a person has, before the
  later of the day on which this section comes into force and the day on
  which a country that is not a treaty country becomes a WCT country,
  incurred an expenditure or a liability in connection with, or in
  preparation for, the doing of an act that, if that country had been a
  WCT country, would have infringed copyright in a work or moral rights
  in respect of a work, any right or interest of that person that arises
  from, or in connection with, the doing of that act and that is
  subsisting and valuable on the later of those days is not, except as
  provided by an order of the Board made under subsection 78(3),
  prejudiced or diminished by reason only of that country having become
  a WCT country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a person's right or interest
  that is protected by that subsection terminates as against the
  copyright owner if and when that owner pays the person any
  compensation that is agreed to between the parties or, failing
  agreement, that is determined by the Board in accordance with section
  78.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 41
:::

## [PART IV]{.HLabel1}[Remedies]{.HTitleText1} {#h-103951 .Part}

### [Civil Remedies]{.HTitleText2} {#h-103952 .Subheading}

#### [Infringement of Copyright and Moral Rights]{.HTitleText3} {#h-103953 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  copyright has been infringed, the owner of the copyright is, subject
  to this Act, entitled to all remedies by way of injunction, damages,
  accounts, delivery up and otherwise that are or may be conferred by
  law for the infringement of a right.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Moral rights

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In any proceedings for an infringement of moral
  rights, the court may grant to the holder of those rights all remedies
  by way of injunction, damages, accounts, delivery up and otherwise
  that are or may be conferred by law for the infringement of a right.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The costs of all parties in any proceedings in
  respect of the infringement of a right conferred by this Act shall be
  in the discretion of the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summary proceedings

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The following proceedings may be commenced or
  proceeded with by way of application or action and shall, in the case
  of an application, be heard and determined without delay and in a
  summary way:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} proceedings for infringement of copyright or moral
    rights;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} proceedings taken under section 44.12, 44.2 or
    44.4; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} proceedings taken in respect of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a tariff approved by the Board under Part VII.1
      or VIII, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} agreements referred to in subsection 67(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Practice and procedure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The rules of practice and procedure, in civil
  matters, of the court in which proceedings are commenced by way of
  application apply to those proceedings, but where those rules do not
  provide for the proceedings to be heard and determined without delay
  and in a summary way, the court may give such directions as it
  considers necessary in order to so provide.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Actions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The court in which proceedings are instituted by way
  of application may, where it considers it appropriate, direct that the
  proceeding be proceeded with as an action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of
  [[application]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[application]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means a proceeding that is commenced other than by way of a writ or
  statement of claim.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 34
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 8
- 1993, c. 15, s. 3(E), c. 44, s. 65
- 1994, c. 47, s. 62
- 1997, c. 24, s. 20
- 2012, c. 20, s. 43
- 2014, c. 32, s. 6
- [2018, c. 27, s. 286](#1039414-1121285){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1039414-1121285.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34-20150101.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumptions respecting copyright and
ownership

- **[[34.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  any civil proceedings taken under this Act in which the defendant puts
  in issue either the existence of the copyright or the title of the
  plaintiff to it,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} copyright shall be presumed, unless the contrary is
    proved, to subsist in the work, performer's performance, sound
    recording or communication signal, as the case may be; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the author, performer, maker or broadcaster, as the
    case may be, shall, unless the contrary is proved, be presumed to be
    the owner of the copyright.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no grant registered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where any matter referred to in subsection (1) is at
  issue and no assignment of the copyright, or licence granting an
  interest in the copyright, has been registered under this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if a name purporting to be that of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the author of the work,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the performer of the performer's performance,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the maker of the sound recording, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the broadcaster of the communication signal

    is printed or otherwise indicated thereon in the usual manner, the
    person whose name is so printed or indicated shall, unless the
    contrary is proved, be presumed to be the author, performer, maker
    or broadcaster;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} no name is so printed or indicated, or if the
      name so printed or indicated is not the true name of the author,
      performer, maker or broadcaster or the name by which that person
      is commonly known, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a name purporting to be that of the publisher or
      owner of the work, performer's performance, sound recording or
      communication signal is printed or otherwise indicated thereon in
      the usual manner,

    the person whose name is printed or indicated as described in
    subparagraph (ii) shall, unless the contrary is proved, be presumed
    to be the owner of the copyright in question; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if, on a cinematographic work, a name purporting to
    be that of the maker of the cinematographic work appears in the
    usual manner, the person so named shall, unless the contrary is
    proved, be presumed to be the maker of the cinematographic work.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 20
- 2012, c. 20, s. 44
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 34.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability for infringement

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person infringes copyright, the person is liable to pay such damages
  to the owner of the copyright as the owner has suffered due to the
  infringement and, in addition to those damages, such part of the
  profits that the infringer has made from the infringement and that
  were not taken into account in calculating the damages as the court
  considers just.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of profits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In proving profits,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff shall be required to prove only
    receipts or revenues derived from the infringement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the defendant shall be required to prove every
    element of cost that the defendant claims.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 35
- 1997, c. 24, s. 20
:::

**[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 20,
s. 45\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-36-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 36"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 20,
s. 45\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-37-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 37"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of possession of copies, plates

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the owner
  of the copyright in a work or other subject-matter may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} recover possession of all infringing copies of that
    work or other subject-matter, and of all plates used or intended to
    be used for the production of infringing copies, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take proceedings for seizure of those copies or
    plates before judgment if, under the law of Canada or of the
    province in which those proceedings are taken, a person is entitled
    to take such proceedings,

  as if those copies or plates were the property of the copyright owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person from whom the copyright owner has
    recovered possession of copies or plates referred to in subsection
    (1),

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person against whom proceedings for seizure
    before judgment of copies or plates referred to in subsection (1)
    have been taken, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other person who has an interest in those
    copies or plates,

  a court may order that those copies or plates be destroyed, or may
  make any other order that it considers appropriate in the
  circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to interested persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before making an order under subsection (2), the
  court shall direct that notice be given to any person who has an
  interest in the copies or plates in question, unless the court is of
  the opinion that the interests of justice do not require such notice
  to be given.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumstances court to consider

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In making an order under subsection (2), the court
  shall have regard to all the circumstances, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the proportion, importance and value of the
    infringing copy or plate, as compared to the substrate or carrier
    embodying it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which the infringing copy or plate is
    severable from, or a distinct part of, the substrate or carrier
    embodying it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Act entitles the copyright owner to
  damages in respect of the possession or conversion of the infringing
  copies or plates.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 38
- 1997, c. 24, s. 20
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statutory damages

- **[[38.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, a copyright
  owner may elect, at any time before final judgment is rendered, to
  recover, instead of damages and profits referred to in subsection
  35(1), an award of statutory damages for which any one infringer is
  liable individually, or for which any two or more infringers are
  liable jointly and severally,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a sum of not less than \$500 and not more than
    \$20,000 that the court considers just, with respect to all
    infringements involved in the proceedings for each work or other
    subject-matter, if the infringements are for commercial purposes;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a sum of not less than \$100 and not more than
    \$5,000 that the court considers just, with respect to all
    infringements involved in the proceedings for all works or other
    subject-matter, if the infringements are for non-commercial
    purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Infringement of subsection 27(2.3)

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} An infringement under subsection 27(2.3) may give
  rise to an award of statutory damages with respect to a work or other
  subject-matter only if the copyright in that work or other
  subject-matter was actually infringed as a result of the use of a
  service referred to in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming  --- infringement of subsection
  27(2.3)

  [(1.11)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), an infringement
  under subsection 27(2.3) is deemed to be for a commercial purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Infringements not involved in
  proceedings

  [(1.12)]{.lawlabel} If the copyright owner has made an election under
  subsection (1) with respect to a defendant's infringements that are
  for non-commercial purposes, they are barred from recovering statutory
  damages under this section from that defendant with respect to any
  other of the defendant's infringements that were done for
  non-commercial purposes before the institution of the proceedings in
  which the election was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No other statutory damages

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If a copyright owner has made an election under
  subsection (1) with respect to a defendant's infringements that are
  for non-commercial purposes, every other copyright owner is barred
  from electing to recover statutory damages under this section in
  respect of that defendant for any of the defendant's infringements
  that were done for non-commercial purposes before the institution of
  the proceedings in which the election was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If defendant unaware of infringement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a copyright owner has made an election under
  subsection (1) and the defendant satisfies the court that the
  defendant was not aware and had no reasonable grounds to believe that
  the defendant had infringed copyright, the court may reduce the amount
  of the award under paragraph (1)(a) to less than \$500, but not less
  than \$200.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special case

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In awarding statutory damages under paragraph (1)(a)
  or subsection (2), the court may award, with respect to each work or
  other subject-matter, a lower amount than \$500 or \$200, as the case
  may be, that the court considers just, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} there is more than one work or other
      subject-matter in a single medium, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the award relates only to one or more
      infringements under subsection 27(2.3); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the awarding of even the minimum amount referred to
    in that paragraph or that subsection would result in a total award
    that, in the court's opinion, is grossly out of proportion to the
    infringement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation --- certain acts

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A collective society or copyright owner who has
  authorized a collective society to act on their behalf may make an
  election under this section with respect to an act set out in
  subsection (4.1) only if applicable royalties are set out in an
  approved tariff or fixed under subsection 71(2) and the defendant has
  not paid them. If they make the election, the collective society or
  copyright owner may only recover, in lieu of any other remedy of a
  monetary nature provided by this Act, an award of statutory damages in
  respect of such acts in a sum of not less than three and not more than
  ten times the amount of the applicable royalties, as the court
  considers just.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acts for the purposes of subsection (4)

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) applies with respect to the
  following acts:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the performance in public of musical works or
    dramatico-musical works, of performer's performances of such works,
    or of sound recordings embodying such works or performances; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the communication to the public by
    telecommunication of musical works or dramatico-musical works, other
    than as described in subsection 31(2), of performer's performances
    of such works, or of sound recordings embodying such works or
    performances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to consider

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In exercising its discretion under subsections (1) to
  (4), the court shall consider all relevant factors, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the good faith or bad faith of the defendant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of the parties before and during the
    proceedings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the need to deter other infringements of the
    copyright in question; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of infringements for non-commercial
    purposes, the need for an award to be proportionate to the
    infringements, in consideration of the hardship the award may cause
    to the defendant, whether the infringement was for private purposes
    or not, and the impact of the infringements on the plaintiff.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No award

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No statutory damages may be awarded against

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an educational institution or a person acting under
    its authority that has committed an act referred to in section 29.6
    or 29.7 and has not paid any royalties or complied with any terms
    and conditions fixed under this Act in relation to the commission of
    the act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an educational institution, library, archive or
    museum that is sued in the circumstances referred to in section
    38.2;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who infringes copyright under paragraph
    27(2)(e) or section 27.1, where the copy in question was made with
    the consent of the copyright owner in the country where the copy was
    made; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an educational institution that is sued in the
    circumstances referred to in subsection 30.02(7) or a person acting
    under its authority who is sued in the circumstances referred to in
    subsection 30.02(8).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemplary or punitive damages not
  affected

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An election under subsection (1) does not affect any
  right that the copyright owner may have to exemplary or punitive
  damages.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 20
- 2012, c. 20, s. 46
- [2018, c. 27, s. 287](#1039416-1121294){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1039416-1121294.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-38.1-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 38.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum amount that may be recovered

- **[[38.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  owner of copyright in a work who has not authorized a collective
  society to authorize its reprographic reproduction may recover, in
  proceedings against an educational institution, library, archive or
  museum that has reproduced the work, a maximum amount equal to the
  amount of royalties that would have been payable to the society in
  respect of the reprographic reproduction, if it were authorized,
  either

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} under any agreement entered into with the
    collective society; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} under a tariff approved by the Board under section
    70.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements with more than one
  collective society

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where agreements respecting reprographic reproduction
  have been signed with more than one collective society or where more
  than one tariff applies or where both agreements and tariffs apply,
  the maximum amount that the copyright owner may recover is the largest
  amount of the royalties provided for in any of those agreements or
  tariffs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) apply only where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the collective society is entitled to authorize, or
    the tariff provides for the payment of royalties in respect of, the
    reprographic reproduction of that category of work; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} copying of that general nature and extent is
    covered by the agreement or tariff.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 20
- [2018, c. 27, s. 288](#1039418-1121295){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1039418-1121295.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-38.2-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 38.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Injunction only remedy when defendant not
aware of copyright

- **[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), in any
  proceedings for infringement of copyright, the plaintiff is not
  entitled to any remedy other than an injunction in respect of the
  infringement if the defendant proves that, at the date of the
  infringement, the defendant was not aware and had no reasonable ground
  for suspecting that copyright subsisted in the work or other
  subject-matter in question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception where copyright registered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if, at the date of the
  infringement, the copyright was duly registered under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 39
- 1997, c. 24, s. 20
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wide injunction

- **[[39.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When granting an injunction in
  respect of an infringement of copyright in a work or other
  subject-matter, the court may further enjoin the defendant from
  infringing the copyright in any other work or subject-matter if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff is the owner of the copyright or the
    person to whom an interest in the copyright has been granted by
    licence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the plaintiff satisfies the court that the
    defendant will likely infringe the copyright in those other works or
    subject-matter unless enjoined by the court from doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of injunction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An injunction granted under subsection (1) may extend
  to works or other subject-matter

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of which the plaintiff was not, at the
    time the proceedings were commenced, the owner of the copyright or
    the person to whom an interest in the copyright has been granted by
    licence; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that did not exist at the time the proceedings were
    commenced.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 20
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No injunction in case of a building

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  the construction of a building or other structure that infringes or
  that, if completed, would infringe the copyright in some other work
  has been commenced, the owner of the copyright is not entitled to
  obtain an injunction in respect of the construction of that building
  or structure or to order its demolition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain remedies inapplicable

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 38 and 42 do not apply in any case in
  respect of which subsection (1) applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 40
- 1997, c. 24, s. 21
:::

#### [Technological Protection Measures and Rights Management Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-104106 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this section and in sections 41.1 to 41.21.

[[circumvent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[circumvent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a technological protection measure
      within the meaning of paragraph (a) of the definition
      [[technological protection measure]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, to
      descramble a scrambled work or computer program, or decrypt an
      encrypted work or computer program or to otherwise avoid, bypass,
      remove, deactivate or impair the technological protection measure,
      unless it is done with the authority of the copyright owner; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a technological protection measure
      within the meaning of paragraph (b) of the definition
      [[technological protection measure]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, to
      avoid, bypass, remove, deactivate or impair the technological
      protection measure. ([contourner]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[technological protection measure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[technological protection measure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
    effective technology, device or component that, in the ordinary
    course of its operation,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} controls access to a work, including a computer
      program, to a performer's performance fixed in a sound recording
      or to a sound recording and whose use is authorized by the
      copyright owner;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} restricts the doing --- with respect to a work,
      to a performer's performance fixed in a sound recording or to a
      sound recording --- of any act referred to in section 3, 15 or 18
      and any act for which remuneration is payable under section
      19. ([mesure technique de protection]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 41
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 9
- 1997, c. 24, s. 22
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
- [2024, c. 26, s. 1](#1494650-1494806){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1494650-1494806.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 41"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[41.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} circumvent a technological protection measure
    within the meaning of paragraph (a) of the definition
    [[technological protection measure]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
    section 41;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} offer services to the public or provide services if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the services are offered or provided primarily
      for the purposes of circumventing a technological protection
      measure,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the uses or purposes of those services are not
      commercially significant other than when they are offered or
      provided for the purposes of circumventing a technological
      protection measure, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the person markets those services as being for
      the purposes of circumventing a technological protection measure
      or acts in concert with another person in order to market those
      services as being for those purposes; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} manufacture, import, distribute, offer for sale or
    rental or provide --- including by selling or renting --- any
    technology, device or component if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the technology, device or component is designed
      or produced primarily for the purposes of circumventing a
      technological protection measure,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the uses or purposes of the technology, device
      or component are not commercially significant other than when it
      is used for the purposes of circumventing a technological
      protection measure, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the person markets the technology, device or
      component as being for the purposes of circumventing a
      technological protection measure or acts in concert with another
      person in order to market the technology, device or component as
      being for those purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumvention of technological
  protection measure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The owner of the copyright in a work, a performer's
  performance fixed in a sound recording or a sound recording in respect
  of which paragraph (1)(a) has been contravened is, subject to this Act
  and any regulations made under section 41.21, entitled to all remedies
  --- by way of injunction, damages, accounts, delivery up and otherwise
  --- that are or may be conferred by law for the infringement of
  copyright against the person who contravened that paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No statutory damages

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The owner of the copyright in a work, a performer's
  performance fixed in a sound recording or a sound recording in respect
  of which paragraph (1)(a) has been contravened may not elect under
  section 38.1 to recover statutory damages from an individual who
  contravened that paragraph only for his or her own private purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services, technology, device or
  component

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every owner of the copyright in a work, a performer's
  performance fixed in a sound recording or a sound recording in respect
  of which a technological protection measure has been or could be
  circumvented as a result of the contravention of paragraph (1)(b)
  or (c) is, subject to this Act and any regulations made under section
  41.21, entitled to all remedies --- by way of injunction, damages,
  accounts, delivery up and otherwise --- that are or may be conferred
  by law for the infringement of copyright against the person who
  contravened paragraph (1)(b) or (c).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Law enforcement and national security

- **[[41.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply
  if a technological protection measure is circumvented for the purposes
  of an investigation related to the enforcement of any Act of
  Parliament or any Act of the legislature of a province, or for the
  purposes of activities related to the protection of national security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(b) does not apply if the services
  are provided by or for the persons responsible for carrying out such
  an investigation or such activities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technology, device or component

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(c) does not apply if the
  technology, device or component is manufactured, imported or provided
  by the persons responsible for carrying out such an investigation or
  such activities, or is manufactured, imported, provided or offered for
  sale or rental as a service provided to those persons.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interoperability

- **[[41.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply
  to a person who circumvents a technological protection measure that
  protects a lawfully obtained computer program for the purpose of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtaining information that would allow the person
    to make the program or a device in which it is embedded
    interoperable with any other computer program, device or component;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} making the program or a device in which it is
    embedded interoperable with any other computer program, device or
    component.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(b) does not apply to a person who
  offers services to the public or provides services for the purposes of
  circumventing a technological protection measure if the person does so
  for the purpose of making the computer program and any other computer
  program interoperable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technology, device or component

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(c) does not apply to a person who
  manufactures, imports or provides a technology, device or component
  for the purposes of circumventing a technological protection measure
  if the person does so for the purpose of making the computer program
  and any other computer program interoperable and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} uses that technology, device or component only for
    that purpose; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provides that technology, device or component to
    another person only for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sharing of information

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person referred to in paragraph (1)(a) may
  communicate the information obtained under that paragraph to another
  person for the purposes of allowing that person to make the computer
  program or a device in which it is embedded interoperable with any
  other computer program, device or component.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation --- technology

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom the technology, device or component
  referred to in subsection (3) is provided may use it only for the
  purpose of making the computer program and any other computer program
  interoperable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation --- information

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom the information referred to in
  subsection (4) is communicated may use it only for the purpose of
  making the computer program or a device in which it is embedded
  interoperable with any other computer program, device or component.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A person is not entitled to benefit from the
