  exceptions under subsection (1) or (6) if, for the purposes of making
  the computer program or a device in which it is embedded interoperable
  with any other computer program, device or component, the person does
  an act that constitutes an infringement of copyright.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A person is not entitled to benefit from the
  exceptions under subsection (2), (3) or (5) if, for the purposes of
  making the computer program and any other computer program
  interoperable, the person does an act that constitutes an infringement
  of copyright.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A person is not entitled to benefit from the
  exception under subsection (4) if, for the purposes of making the
  computer program or a device in which it is embedded interoperable
  with any other computer program, device or component, the person does
  an act that constitutes an infringement of copyright or an act that
  contravenes any Act of Parliament or any Act of the legislature of a
  province.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
- [2024, c. 27, s. 1](#1494368-1494860){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1494368-1494860.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41.12-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 41.12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Diagnosis, maintenance and repair

- **[[41.121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.121
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply
  to a person who circumvents a technological protection measure for the
  sole purpose of maintaining or repairing a product, including any
  related diagnosing, if the work, performer's performance fixed in a
  sound recording or sound recording to which the technological
  protection measure controls access forms a part of the product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection (1) applies to a
  person who circumvents a technological protection measure in the
  circumstances referred to in that subsection for another person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person acting in the circumstances referred to in
  subsection (1) is not entitled to benefit from the exception under
  that subsection if the person does an act that constitutes an
  infringement of copyright.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 26, s. 2](#1494655-1494821){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1494655-1494821.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Encryption research

- **[[41.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply
  to a person who, for the purposes of encryption research, circumvents
  a technological protection measure by means of decryption if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it would not be practical to carry out the research
    without circumventing the technological protection measure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person has lawfully obtained the work, the
    performer's performance fixed in a sound recording or the sound
    recording that is protected by the technological protection measure;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person has informed the owner of the copyright
    in the work, the performer's performance fixed in a sound recording
    or the sound recording who has applied the technological protection
    measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, a person acting in the circumstances
  referred to in subsection (1) is not entitled to benefit from the
  exception under that subsection if the person does an act that
  constitutes an infringement of copyright or an act that contravenes
  any Act of Parliament or any Act of the legislature of a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technology, device or component

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(c) does not apply to a person
  referred to in subsection (1) who manufactures a technology, device or
  component for the purposes of circumventing a technological protection
  measure that is subject to paragraph 41.1(1)(a) if the person does so
  for the purpose of encryption research and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} uses that technology, device or component only for
    that purpose; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provides that technology, device or component only
    for that purpose to another person who is collaborating with the
    person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal information

- **[[41.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply
  to a person who circumvents a technological protection measure if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the work, performer's performance fixed in a sound
    recording or sound recording that is protected by the technological
    protection measure is not accompanied by a notice indicating that
    its use will permit a third party to collect and communicate
    personal information relating to the user or, in the case where it
    is accompanied by such a notice, the user is not provided with the
    option to prevent the collection and communication of personal
    information without the user's use of it being restricted; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the only purpose of circumventing the technological
    protection measure is to verify whether it permits the collection or
    communication of personal information and, if it does, to prevent
    it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services, technology, device or
  component

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 41.1(1)(b) and (c) do not apply to a
  person who offers services to the public or provides services, or
  manufactures, imports or provides a technology, device or component,
  for the purposes of circumventing a technological protection measure
  in accordance with subsection (1), to the extent that the services,
  technology, device or component do not unduly impair the technological
  protection measure.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security

- **[[41.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply
  to a person who circumvents a technological protection measure that is
  subject to that paragraph for the sole purpose of, with the consent of
  the owner or administrator of a computer, computer system or computer
  network, assessing the vulnerability of the computer, system or
  network or correcting any security flaws.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(b) does not apply if the services
  are provided to a person described in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technology, device or component

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(c) does not apply if the
  technology, device or component is manufactured or imported by a
  person described in subsection (1), or is manufactured, imported,
  provided --- including by selling or renting --- offered for sale or
  rental or distributed as a service provided to that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person acting in the circumstances referred to in
  subsection (1) is not entitled to benefit from the exception under
  that subsection if the person does an act that constitutes an
  infringement of copyright or an act that contravenes any Act of
  Parliament or any Act of the legislature of a province.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons with perceptual disabilities

- **[[41.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply
  to a person with a perceptual disability, to another person acting at
  their request or to a non-profit organization, as defined in section
  32.02, acting for their benefit, if that person or organization
  circumvents a technological protection measure solely for one or more
  of the following purposes:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to make a work, a performer's performance fixed in
    a sound recording or a sound recording perceptible to the person
    with a perceptual disability;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to permit a person, or a non-profit organization
    referred to in subsection 32(1), to benefit from the exception set
    out in section 32;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to permit a non-profit organization referred to in
    subsection 32.01(1) to benefit from the exception set out in section
    32.01.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services, technology, device or
  component

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 41.1(1)(b) and (c) do not apply to a
  person who offers or provides services to persons or non-profit
  organizations referred to in subsection (1) or who manufactures,
  imports or provides a technology, device or component, for the sole
  purpose of enabling those persons or non-profit organizations to
  circumvent a technological protection measure in accordance with that
  subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
- 2016, c. 4, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41.16-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 41.16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Broadcasting undertakings

**[[41.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.17 .sectionLabel}** Paragraph
41.1(1)(a) does not apply to a broadcasting undertaking that circumvents
a technological protection measure for the sole purpose of making an
ephemeral reproduction of a work, a performer's performance fixed in a
sound recording or a sound recording in accordance with section 30.9,
unless the owner of the copyright in the work, the performer's
performance fixed in a sound recording or the sound recording that is
protected by the technological protection measure makes available the
necessary means to enable the making of such a reproduction in a timely
manner in light of the broadcasting undertaking's business requirements.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Radio apparatus

- **[[41.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply
  to a person who circumvents a technological protection measure on a
  radio apparatus for the sole purpose of gaining access to a
  telecommunications service by means of the radio apparatus.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Services or technology, device or
  component

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 41.1(1)(b) and (c) do not apply to a
  person who offers the services to the public or provides the services,
  or manufactures, imports or provides the technology, device or
  component, for the sole purpose of facilitating access to a
  telecommunications service by means of a radio apparatus.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in this section.

  [[radio apparatus]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[radio apparatus]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      section 2 of the [Radiocommunication
      Act](/eng/acts/R-2). ([appareil radio]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[telecommunications service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[telecommunications service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same
      meaning as in subsection 2(1) of the [Telecommunications
      Act](/eng/acts/T-3.4). ([service de
      télécommunication]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction of damages

**[[41.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.19 .sectionLabel}** A court may reduce
or remit the amount of damages it awards in the circumstances described
in subsection 41.1(1) if the defendant satisfies the court that the
defendant was not aware, and had no reasonable grounds to believe, that
the defendant's acts constituted a contravention of that subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Injunction only remedy

**[[41.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.2 .sectionLabel}** If a court finds that
a defendant that is a library, archive or museum or an educational
institution has contravened subsection 41.1(1) and the defendant
satisfies the court that it was not aware, and had no reasonable grounds
to believe, that its actions constituted a contravention of that
subsection, the plaintiff is not entitled to any remedy other than an
injunction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[41.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make
  regulations excluding from the application of section 41.1 any
  technological protection measure that protects a work, a performer's
  performance fixed in a sound recording or a sound recording, or
  classes of them, or any class of such technological protection
  measures, if the Governor in Council considers that the application of
  that section to the technological protection measure or class of
  technological protection measures would unduly restrict competition in
  the aftermarket sector in which the technological protection measure
  is used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing additional circumstances in which
    paragraph 41.1(1)(a) does not apply, having regard to the following
    factors:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} whether not being permitted to circumvent a
      technological protection measure that is subject to that paragraph
      could adversely affect the use a person may make of a work, a
      performer's performance fixed in a sound recording or a sound
      recording when that use is authorized,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} whether the work, the performer's performance
      fixed in a sound recording or the sound recording is commercially
      available,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} whether not being permitted to circumvent a
      technological protection measure that is subject to that paragraph
      could adversely affect criticism, review, news reporting,
      commentary, parody, satire, teaching, scholarship or research that
      could be made or done in respect of the work, the performer's
      performance fixed in a sound recording or the sound recording,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} whether being permitted to circumvent a
      technological protection measure that is subject to that paragraph
      could adversely affect the market for the work, the performer's
      performance fixed in a sound recording or the sound recording or
      its market value,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} whether the work, the performer's performance
      fixed in a sound recording or the sound recording is commercially
      available in a medium and in a quality that is appropriate for
      non-profit archival, preservation or educational uses, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} requiring the owner of the copyright in a work, a
    performer's performance fixed in a sound recording or a sound
    recording that is protected by a technological protection measure to
    provide access to the work, performer's performance fixed in a sound
    recording or sound recording to persons who are entitled to the
    benefit of any of the limitations on the application of paragraph
    41.1(1)(a) prescribed under paragraph (a). The regulations may
    prescribe the manner in which, and the time within which, access is
    to be provided, as well as any conditions that the owner of the
    copyright is to comply with.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- rights management
information

- **[[41.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No person shall knowingly remove or
  alter any rights management information in electronic form without the
  consent of the owner of the copyright in the work, the performer's
  performance or the sound recording, if the person knows or should have
  known that the removal or alteration will facilitate or conceal any
  infringement of the owner's copyright or adversely affect the owner's
  right to remuneration under section 19.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal or alteration of rights
  management information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The owner of the copyright in a work, a performer's
  performance fixed in a sound recording or a sound recording is,
  subject to this Act, entitled to all remedies --- by way of
  injunction, damages, accounts, delivery up and otherwise --- that are
  or may be conferred by law for the infringement of copyright against a
  person who contravenes subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent acts

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The copyright owner referred to in subsection (2) has
  the same remedies against a person who, without the owner's consent,
  knowingly does any of the following acts with respect to any material
  form of the work, the performer's performance fixed in a sound
  recording or the sound recording and knows or should have known that
  the rights management information has been removed or altered in a way
  that would give rise to a remedy under that subsection:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sells it or rents it out;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} distributes it to an extent that the copyright
    owner is prejudicially affected;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by way of trade, distributes it, exposes or offers
    it for sale or rental or exhibits it in public;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} imports it into Canada for the purpose of doing
    anything referred to in any of paragraphs (a) to (c); or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} communicates it to the public by telecommunication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[rights management
  information]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[rights management
  information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means information that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is attached to or embodied in a copy of a work, a
    performer's performance fixed in a sound recording or a sound
    recording, or appears in connection with its communication to the
    public by telecommunication; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identifies or permits the identification of the
    work or its author, the performance or its performer, the sound
    recording or its maker or the holder of any rights in the work, the
    performance or the sound recording, or concerns the terms or
    conditions of the work's, performance's or sound recording's use.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

#### [General Provisions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-104265 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of separate rights

- **[[41.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, the owner
  of any copyright, or any person or persons deriving any right, title
  or interest by assignment or grant in writing from the owner, may
  individually for himself or herself, as a party to the proceedings in
  his or her own name, protect and enforce any right that he or she
  holds, and, to the extent of that right, title and interest, is
  entitled to the remedies provided by this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright owner to be made party

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If proceedings under subsection (1) are taken by a
  person other than the copyright owner, the copyright owner shall be
  made a party to those proceedings, except

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of proceedings taken under section
    44.12, 44.2 or 44.4;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of interlocutory proceedings, unless
    the court is of the opinion that the interests of justice require
    the copyright owner to be a party; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case in which the court is of the
    opinion that the interests of justice do not require the copyright
    owner to be a party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Owner's liability for costs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A copyright owner who is made a party to proceedings
  under subsection (2) is not liable for any costs unless the copyright
  owner takes part in the proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Apportionment of damages, profits

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a copyright owner is made a party to proceedings
  under subsection (2), the court, in awarding damages or profits,
  shall, subject to any agreement between the person who took the
  proceedings and the copyright owner, apportion the damages or profits
  referred to in subsection 35(1) between them as the court considers
  appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
- 2014, c. 32, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41.23-20121107.html "Link to previous version of section 41.23"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concurrent jurisdiction of Federal Court

**[[41.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.24 .sectionLabel}** The Federal Court
has concurrent jurisdiction with provincial courts to hear and determine
all proceedings, other than the prosecution of offences under sections
42 and 43, for the enforcement of a provision of this Act or of the
civil remedies provided by this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
:::

### [Provisions Respecting Providers of Network Services or Information Location Tools]{.HTitleText2} {#h-104284 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of claimed infringement

- **[[41.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An owner of the copyright in a work
  or other subject-matter may send a notice of claimed infringement to a
  person who provides

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the means, in the course of providing services
    related to the operation of the Internet or another digital network,
    of telecommunication through which the electronic location that is
    the subject of the claim of infringement is connected to the
    Internet or another digital network;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose set out in subsection 31.1(4), the
    digital memory that is used for the electronic location to which the
    claim of infringement relates; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an information location tool as defined in
    subsection 41.27(5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form and content of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of claimed infringement shall be in writing
  in the form, if any, prescribed by regulation and shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} state the claimant's name and address and any other
    particulars prescribed by regulation that enable communication with
    the claimant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify the work or other subject-matter to which
    the claimed infringement relates;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} state the claimant's interest or right with respect
    to the copyright in the work or other subject-matter;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} specify the location data for the electronic
    location to which the claimed infringement relates;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} specify the infringement that is claimed;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} specify the date and time of the commission of the
    claimed infringement; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} contain any other information that may be
    prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibited content

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A notice of claimed infringement shall not contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offer to settle the claimed infringement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a request or demand, made in relation to the
    claimed infringement, for payment or for personal information;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a reference, including by way of hyperlink, to such
    an offer, request or demand; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other information that may be prescribed by
    regulation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
- 2018, c. 27, s. 243
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41.25-20150102.html "Link to previous version of section 41.25"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations related to notice

- **[[41.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person described in paragraph
  41.25(1)(a) or (b) who receives a notice of claimed infringement that
  complies with subsections 41.25(2) and (3) shall, on being paid any
  fee that the person has lawfully charged for doing so,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as soon as feasible forward the notice
    electronically to the person to whom the electronic location
    identified by the location data specified in the notice belongs and
    inform the claimant of its forwarding or, if applicable, of the
    reason why it was not possible to forward it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} retain records that will allow the identity of the
    person to whom the electronic location belongs to be determined, and
    do so for six months beginning on the day on which the notice of
    claimed infringement is received or, if the claimant commences
    proceedings relating to the claimed infringement and so notifies the
    person before the end of those six months, for one year after the
    day on which the person receives the notice of claimed infringement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees related to notices

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by regulation, fix the maximum fee
  that a person may charge for performing his or her obligations under
  subsection (1). If no maximum is fixed by regulation, the person may
  not charge any amount under that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Damages related to notices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A claimant's only remedy against a person who fails
  to perform his or her obligations under subsection (1) is statutory
  damages in an amount that the court considers just, but not less than
  \$5,000 and not more than \$10,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- change of amounts

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by regulation, increase
  or decrease the minimum or maximum amount of statutory damages set out
  in subsection (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
- 2018, c. 27, s. 244
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41.26-20150102.html "Link to previous version of section 41.26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Injunctive relief only --- providers of
information location tools

- **[[41.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41.27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In any proceedings for infringement
  of copyright, the owner of the copyright in a work or other
  subject-matter is not entitled to any remedy other than an injunction
  against a provider of an information location tool that is found to
  have infringed copyright by making a reproduction of the work or other
  subject-matter or by communicating that reproduction to the public by
  telecommunication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies only if the provider, in
  respect of the work or other subject-matter,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes and caches, or does any act similar to
    caching, the reproduction in an automated manner for the purpose of
    providing the information location tool;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} communicates that reproduction to the public by
    telecommunication for the purpose of providing the information that
    has been located by the information location tool;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} does not modify the reproduction, other than for
    technical reasons;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} complies with any conditions relating to the making
    or caching, or doing of any act similar to caching, of reproductions
    of the work or other subject-matter, or to the communication of the
    reproductions to the public by telecommunication, that were
    specified in a manner consistent with industry practice by whoever
    made the work or other subject-matter available through the Internet
    or another digital network and that lend themselves to automated
    reading and execution; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} does not interfere with the use of technology that
    is lawful and consistent with industry practice in order to obtain
    data on the use of the work or other subject-matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the provider receives a notice of claimed
  infringement, relating to a work or other subject-matter, that
  complies with subsections 41.25(2) and (3) after the work or other
  subject-matter has been removed from the electronic location set out
  in the notice, then subsection (1) applies, with respect to
  reproductions made from that electronic location, only to
  infringements that occurred before the day that is 30 days --- or the
  period that may be prescribed by regulation --- after the day on which
  the provider receives the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to the provision of the
  information location tool if the provision of that tool constitutes an
  infringement of copyright under subsection 27(2.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors  ---  scope of injunction

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} If it grants an injunction as set out in subsection
  (1), the court shall, among any other relevant factors, consider the
  following in establishing the terms of the injunction:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the harm likely to be suffered by the copyright
    owner if steps are not taken to prevent or restrain the
    infringement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the burden imposed on the provider and on the
    operation of the information location tool, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate effect of the injunction and any
      injunctions from other proceedings,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} whether implementing the injunction would be
      technically feasible and effective in addressing the infringement,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} whether implementing the injunction would
      interfere with the use of the information location tool for
      non-infringing acts, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the availability of less burdensome and
      comparably effective means of preventing or restraining the
      infringement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4.2)]{.lawlabel} A court is not permitted to grant an injunction
  under section 39.1 against a provider who is the subject of an
  injunction set out in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of [[information location
  tool]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[information location
  tool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any tool that makes it possible to
  locate information that is available through the Internet or another
  digital network.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 20, s. 47
- 2018, c. 27, s. 245
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-41.27-20150102.html "Link to previous version of section 41.27"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Criminal Remedies]{.HTitleText2} {#h-104349 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person commits an offence who knowingly

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes for sale or rental an infringing copy of a
    work or other subject-matter in which copyright subsists;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sells or rents out, or by way of trade exposes or
    offers for sale or rental, an infringing copy of a work or other
    subject-matter in which copyright subsists;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} distributes infringing copies of a work or other
    subject-matter in which copyright subsists, either for the purpose
    of trade or to such an extent as to affect prejudicially the owner
    of the copyright;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} by way of trade exhibits in public an infringing
    copy of a work or other subject-matter in which copyright subsists;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} possesses, for sale, rental, distribution for the
    purpose of trade or exhibition in public by way of trade, an
    infringing copy of a work or other subject-matter in which copyright
    subsists;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} imports, for sale or rental, into Canada any
    infringing copy of a work or other subject-matter in which copyright
    subsists; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} exports or attempts to export, for sale or rental,
    an infringing copy of a work or other subject-matter in which
    copyright subsists.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession and performance offences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who knowingly

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes or possesses any plate that is specifically
    designed or adapted for the purpose of making infringing copies of
    any work or other subject-matter in which copyright subsists; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for private profit causes to be performed in
    public, without the consent of the owner of the copyright, any work
    or other subject-matter in which copyright subsists.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who commits an offence under
  subsection (1) or (2) is liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine of not more
    than \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than five
    years or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
    \$25,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than six months
    or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of court to deal with copies or
  plates

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court before which any proceedings under this
  section are taken may, on conviction, order that all copies of the
  work or other subject-matter that appear to it to be infringing
  copies, or all plates in the possession of the offender predominantly
  used for making infringing copies, be destroyed or delivered up to the
  owner of the copyright or otherwise dealt with as the court may think
  fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3.01)]{.lawlabel} Before making an order under subsection (3), the
  court shall require that notice be given to the owner of the copies or
  plates and to any other person who, in the court's opinion, appears to
  have a right or interest in them, unless the court is of the opinion
  that the interests of justice do not require that the notice be given.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumvention of technological
  protection measure

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} Every person, except a person who is acting on
  behalf of a library, archive or museum or an educational institution,
  is guilty of an offence who knowingly and for commercial purposes
  contravenes section 41.1 and is liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine not
    exceeding \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    five years or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$25,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or
    to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence --- infringement related to
  rights management information

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} Every person, except a person who is acting on
  behalf of a library, archive or museum or an educational institution,
  commits an offence who knowingly and for commercial purposes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} removes or alters any rights management information
    in electronic form without the consent of the owner of the copyright
    in the work, the performer's performance fixed in a sound recording
    or the sound recording, if the person knows that the removal or
    alteration will facilitate or conceal any infringement of the
    owner's copyright or adversely affect the owner's right to
    remuneration under section 19; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} does any of the acts referred to in paragraphs
    41.22(3)(a) to (e) with respect to any material form of the work,
    the performer's performance fixed in a sound recording or the sound
    recording without the consent of the owner of the copyright and
    knows that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the rights management information in electronic
      form has been removed or altered without the consent of the owner
      of the copyright, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the removal or alteration will facilitate or
      conceal any infringement of the owner's copyright or adversely
      affect the owner's right to remuneration under section 19.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(3.3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who commits an offence under
  subsection (3.2) is liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment to a fine of not more
    than \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than five
    years or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction to a fine of not more than
    \$25,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than six months
    or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[rights management
  information]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3.4)]{.lawlabel} In subsection (3.2), [[rights management
  information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
  subsection 41.22(4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings by summary conviction in respect of an
  offence under this section may be instituted at any time within, but
  not later than, two years after the time when the offence was
  committed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parallel importation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a copy of a work or
  other subject-matter is not infringing if the copy was made with the
  consent of the owner of the copyright in the country where the copy
  was made.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 42
- R.S., 1985, c. 10 (4th Supp.), s. 10
- 1997, c. 24, s. 24
- 2012, c. 20, s. 48
- 2014, c. 32, s. 4
- [2020, c. 1, s. 30](#1237554-1253494){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237554-1253494.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-42-20141209.html "Link to previous version of section 42"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Infringement in case of dramatic,
operatic or musical work

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person who, without the written consent of the owner of the copyright
  or of the legal representative of the owner, knowingly performs or
  causes to be performed in public and for private profit the whole or
  any part, constituting an infringement, of any dramatic or operatic
  work or musical composition in which copyright subsists in Canada is
  guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine not
  exceeding two hundred and fifty dollars and, in the case of a second
  or subsequent offence, either to that fine or to imprisonment for a
  term not exceeding two months or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change or suppression of title or
  author's name

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person who makes or causes to be made any change
  in or suppression of the title, or the name of the author, of any
  dramatic or operatic work or musical composition in which copyright
  subsists in Canada, or who makes or causes to be made any change in
  the work or composition itself without the written consent of the
  author or of his legal representative, in order that the work or
  composition may be performed in whole or in part in public for private
  profit, is guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a
  fine not exceeding five hundred dollars and, in the case of a second
  or subsequent offence, either to that fine or to imprisonment for a
  term not exceeding four months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. C-30, s. 26
:::

### [Limitation or Prescription Period]{.HTitleText2} {#h-104389 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation or prescription period for
civil remedies

- **[[43.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a court
  may award a remedy for any act or omission that has been done contrary
  to this Act only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the proceedings for the act or omission giving rise
    to a remedy are commenced within three years after it occurred, in
    the case where the plaintiff knew, or could reasonably have been
    expected to know, of the act or omission at the time it occurred; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the proceedings for the act or omission giving rise
    to a remedy are commenced within three years after the time when the
    plaintiff first knew of it, or could reasonably have been expected
    to know of it, in the case where the plaintiff did not know, and
    could not reasonably have been expected to know, of the act or
    omission at the time it occurred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court shall apply the limitation or prescription
  period set out in paragraph (1)(a) or (b) only in respect of a party
  who pleads a limitation period.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1994, c. 47, s. 64
- 1997, c. 24, s. 25
- 2012, c. 20, s. 49
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-43.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 43.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Importation and Exportation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-104399 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-104400 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in sections 44.02 to 44.4.

[[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Federal Court or the
    superior court of a province. ([tribunal]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[customs officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[customs officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned by
    the definition [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 2(1)
    of the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6). ([agent des
    douanes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[duties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[duties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in subsection
    2(1) of the [Customs
    Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6). ([droits]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of Public Safety
    and Emergency Preparedness. ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    subsection 2(1) of the [Customs
    Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6). ([dédouane­ment]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[working day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[working day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a day other than a
    Saturday or a holiday. ([jour ouvrable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 44
- R.S., 1985, c. 41 (3rd Supp.), s. 116
- 1997, c. 36, s. 205
- 1999, c. 17, s. 119
- 2005, c. 38, s. 139
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 44"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Prohibition and Detention by Customs Officer]{.HTitleText3} {#h-104411 .Subheading}

##### [Prohibition]{.HTitleText4} {#h-104412 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition on importation or exportation

- **[[44.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Copies of a work or other
  subject-matter in which copyright subsists shall not be imported or
  exported if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they were made without the consent of the owner of
    the copyright in the country where they were made; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they infringe copyright or, if they were not made
    in Canada, they would infringe copyright had they been made in
    Canada by the person who made them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to copies that are
  imported or exported by an individual in their possession or baggage
  if the circumstances, including the number of copies, indicate that
  the copies are intended only for their personal use.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
- [2020, c. 1, s. 31](#1237556-1253497){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237556-1253497.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44.01-20150101.html "Link to previous version of section 44.01"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Request for Assistance]{.HTitleText4} {#h-104424 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for assistance

- **[[44.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The owner of copyright in a work or
  other subject-matter may file with the Minister, in the form and
  manner specified by the Minister, a request for assistance in pursuing
  remedies under this Act with respect to copies imported or exported in
  contravention of section 44.01.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information in request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The request for assistance shall include the
  copyright owner's name and address in Canada and any other information
  that is required by the Minister, including information about the work
  or other subject-matter in question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A request for assistance is valid for a period of two
  years beginning on the day on which it is accepted by the Minister.
  The Minister may, at the request of the copyright owner, extend the
  period for two years, and may do so more than once.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, as a condition of accepting a
  request for assistance or of extending a request's period of validity,
  require that the copyright owner furnish security, in an amount and
  form fixed by the Minister, for the payment of an amount for which the
  copyright owner becomes liable under section 44.07.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Update

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The copyright owner shall inform the Minister in
  writing, as soon as practicable, of any changes to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the subsistence of the copyright that is the
    subject of the request for assistance; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the ownership of that copyright.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

##### [Measures Relating to Detained Copies]{.HTitleText4} {#h-104440 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of information by customs
officer

**[[44.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.03 .sectionLabel}** A customs officer
who is detaining copies of a work or other subject-matter under section
101 of the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) may, in the officer's
discretion, to obtain information about whether the importation or
exportation of the copies is prohibited under section 44.01, provide the
owner of copyright in that work or subject-matter with a sample of the
copies and with any information about the copies that the customs
officer reasonably believes does not directly or indirectly identify any
person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of information to pursue remedy

- **[[44.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A customs officer who is detaining
  copies of a work or other subject-matter under section 101 of the
  [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) and who has reasonable grounds to
  suspect that the importation or exportation of the copies is
  prohibited under section 44.01 may, in the officer's discretion, if
  the Minister has accepted a request for assistance with respect to the
  work or subject-matter filed by the owner of copyright in it, provide
  that owner with a sample of the copies and with information about the
  copies that could assist them in pursuing a remedy under this Act,
  such as

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the copies and of their
    characteristics;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of their owner, importer,
    exporter and consignee and of the person who made them and of any
    other person involved in their movement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} their quantity;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the countries in which they were made and through
    which they passed in transit; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the day on which they were imported, if applicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the customs officer shall
  not detain, for the purpose of enforcing section 44.01, the copies for
  more than 10 working days  ---  or, if the copies are perishable, for
  more than five days  ---  after the day on which the customs officer
  first sends or makes available a sample or information to the
  copyright owner under subsection (1). At the request of the copyright
  owner made while the copies are detained for the purpose of enforcing
  section 44.01, the customs officer may, having regard to the
  circumstances, detain non-perishable copies for one additional period
  of not more than 10 working days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of proceedings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, before the copies are no longer detained for the
  purpose of enforcing section 44.01, the owner of copyright has
  provided the Minister, in the manner specified by the Minister, with a
  copy of a document filed with a court commencing proceedings to obtain
  a remedy under this Act with respect to the detained copies, the
  customs officer shall continue to detain them until the Minister is
  informed in writing that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the proceedings are finally disposed of, settled or
    abandoned;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a court directs that the copies are no longer to be
    detained for the purpose of the proceedings; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the copyright owner consents to the copies no
    longer being so detained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued detention

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The occurrence of any of the events referred to in
  paragraphs (3)(a) to (c) does not preclude a customs officer from
  continuing to detain the copies under the [Customs
  Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) for a purpose other than the proceedings.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
- [2020, c. 1, s. 32](#1237560-1253499){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1237560-1253499.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44.04-20150101.html "Link to previous version of section 44.04"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on information use  ---
section 44.03

- **[[44.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.05
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who receives a sample or
  information that is provided under section 44.03 shall not use the
  information, or information that is derived from the sample, for any
  purpose other than to give information to the customs officer about
  whether the importation or exportation of the copies is prohibited
  under section 44.01.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on information use  ---
  subsection 44.04(1)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who receives a sample or information that is
  provided under subsection 44.04(1) shall not use the information, or
  information that is derived from the sample, for any purpose other
  than to pursue remedies under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection (2) does not
  prevent the confidential communication of information about the copies
  for the purpose of reaching an out-of-court settlement.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection

**[[44.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.06 .sectionLabel}** After a sample or
information has been provided under subsection 44.04(1), a customs
officer may, in the officer's discretion, give the owner, importer,
exporter and consignee of the detained copies and the owner of copyright
an opportunity to inspect the copies.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability for charges

- **[[44.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.07
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The owner of copyright who has
  received a sample or information under subsection 44.04(1) is liable
  to Her Majesty in right of Canada for the storage and handling charges
  for the detained copies  ---  and, if applicable, for the charges for
  destroying them  ---  for the period beginning on the day after the
  day on which a customs officer first sends or makes available a sample
  or information to that owner under that subsection and ending on the
  first day on which one of the following occurs:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the copies are no longer detained for the purpose
    of enforcing section 44.01 or, if subsection 44.04(3) applies, for
    the purpose of the proceedings referred to in that subsection;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister receives written notification in which
    the owner states that the importation or exportation of the copies
    does not, with respect to the owner's copyright, contravene section
    44.01;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Minister receives written notification in which
    the owner states that they will not, while the copies are detained
    for the purpose of enforcing section 44.01, commence proceedings to
    obtain a remedy under this Act with respect to them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception   --- paragraph (1)(a)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (1)(a), if the copies are forfeited
  under subsection 39(1) of the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) and the
  Minister did not, before the end of the detention of the copies for
  the purpose of enforcing section 44.01, receive a copy of a document
  filed with a court commencing proceedings to obtain a remedy under
  this Act with respect to the detained copies or the written
  notification referred to in paragraph (1)(b) or (c), the period ends
  on the day on which the copies are forfeited.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception  --- paragraph (1)(c)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (1)(c), if the copies are forfeited
  under subsection 39(1) of the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) after
  the Minister has received the written notification referred to in that
  paragraph, the period ends on the day on which the copies are
  forfeited.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint and several or solidary liability

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The owner and the importer or exporter of copies that
  are forfeited in the circumstances set out in subsection (2) or (3)
  are jointly and severally, or solidarily, liable to the owner of
  copyright for all the charges under subsection (1) paid by the
  copyright owner with respect to the period

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the circumstances referred to in subsection (2),
    beginning on the day on which the copies are no longer detained for
    the purpose of enforcing section 44.01 and ending on the day on
    which the copies are forfeited; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the circumstances referred to in subsection (3),
    beginning on the day on which the Minister receives the written
    notification referred to in paragraph (1)(c) and ending on the day
    on which the copies are forfeited.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) to (3) do not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the detention of the copies for the purpose of
    enforcing section 44.01 ends before the expiry of 10 working days 
    ---  or, if the copies are perishable, before the expiry of five
    days  ---  after the day on which the customs officer first sends or
    makes available a sample or information to the copyright owner under
    subsection 44.04(1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister has not, by the end of the detention,
    received a copy of a document filed with a court commencing
    proceedings to obtain a remedy under this Act with respect to the
    detained copies or the written notification referred to in paragraph
    (1)(b) or (c).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

##### [No Liability]{.HTitleText4} {#h-104497 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No liability

**[[44.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.08 .sectionLabel}** Neither Her Majesty
nor a customs officer is liable for any loss or damage suffered in
relation to the enforcement or application of sections 44.01 to 44.04
and 44.06 because of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the detention of copies of a work or other
  subject-matter, except if the detention contravenes subsection
  44.04(2);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the failure to detain copies; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the release or cessation of detention of any copies,
  except if the release or cessation contravenes subsection 44.04(3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

##### [Powers of Court Relating to Detained Copies]{.HTitleText4} {#h-104505 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court

- **[[44.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.09
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In the course of proceedings
  referred to in subsection 44.04(3), the court may, on the application
  of the Minister or a party to the proceedings,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} impose conditions on the storage or detention of
    the copies that are the subject of the proceedings; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} direct that the copies are no longer to be detained
    for the purpose of the proceedings, on any conditions that the court
    may impose, if their owner, importer, exporter or consignee
    furnishes security in an amount fixed by the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's consent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a party applies to have the detained copies stored
  in a place other than a bonded warehouse or a sufferance warehouse, as
  those terms are defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Customs
  Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6), the Minister must consent to the storage of
  the copies in that place before a condition to that effect is imposed
  under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court may impose a condition described in
  subsection (2) despite section 31 of the [Customs
  Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued detention

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A direction under paragraph (1)(b) that the copies
  are no longer to be detained for the purpose of the proceedings does
  not preclude a customs officer from continuing to detain the copies
  under the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) for another purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In the course of proceedings referred to in
  subsection 44.04(3), the court may, on the application of the Minister
  or a party to the proceedings, require the owner of copyright to
  furnish security, in an amount fixed by the court,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to cover duties, storage and handling charges, and
    any other amount that may become chargeable against the copies; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to answer any damages that may, because of the
    detention of the copies, be sustained by the owner, importer,
    exporter or consignee of the copies.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Damages against copyright owner

- **[[44.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The court may award damages against
  the owner of copyright who commenced proceedings referred to in
  subsection 44.04(3) to the owner, importer, exporter or consignee of
  the copies who is a party to the proceedings for losses, costs or
  prejudice suffered as a result of the detention of the copies if the
  proceedings are dismissed or discontinued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Damages awarded to copyright owner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any damages under subsection 34(1) awarded to the
  owner of copyright in proceedings referred to in subsection 44.04(3)
  are to include the charges incurred by the copyright owner as a result
  of storing, handling or, if applicable, destroying the detained
  copies.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 66
- 1997, c. 24, s. 27
- 2005, c. 38, ss. 142, 145
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44.1-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 44.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Prohibition Resulting from Notice]{.HTitleText3} {#h-104530 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Importation of certain copyright works
prohibited

**[[44.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.11 .sectionLabel}** Copies made outside
Canada of any work in which copyright subsists that if made in Canada
would infringe copyright and as to which the owner of the copyright
gives notice in writing to the Canada Border Services Agency that the
owner desires that the copies not be imported into Canada, shall not be
so imported and are deemed to be included in tariff item No. 9897.00.00
in the List of Tariff Provisions set out in the schedule to the [Customs
Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011), with section 136 of that Act applying
accordingly.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

#### [Court-ordered Detention]{.HTitleText3} {#h-104535 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of court

- **[[44.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A court may make an order described
  in subsection (3) if the court is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} copies of the work are about to be imported into
    Canada, or have been imported into Canada but have not been
    released;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the copies were either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} made without the consent of the person who is
      owner of the copyright in the country where they were made, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} made elsewhere than in a country to which this
      Act extends; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the copies would infringe copyright if they were
    made in Canada by the importer and the importer knows or should have
    known this.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who may apply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A court may make an order described in subsection (3)
  on application by the owner of the copyright in a work in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of court

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In an order made under subsection (1), the court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} direct the Minister

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to take reasonable measures, on the basis of
      information reasonably required by the Minister and provided by
      the applicant, to detain the copies of the work, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to notify the applicant and the importer,
      immediately after detaining the copies of the work, of the
      detention and the reasons for the detention; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide for any other matters that the court
    considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How application made

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An application for an order under subsection (1) may
  be made in an action or otherwise, and either on notice or [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}, except that it must always be made on
  notice to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Before making an order under subsection (1), the
  court may require the applicant to furnish security, in an amount
  fixed by the court,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to cover duties, storage and handling charges and
    any other amount that may become chargeable against the copies of
    the work; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to answer any damages that may by reason of the
    order be incurred by the owner, importer or consignee of the work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for directions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may apply to the court for directions in
  implementing an order made under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may allow inspection

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may give the applicant or the importer
  an opportunity to inspect the detained copies of the work for the
  purpose of substantiating or refuting, as the case may be, the
  applicant's claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If applicant fails to commence action

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Unless an order made under subsection (1) provides
  otherwise, the Minister shall, subject to the [Customs
  Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) and to any other Act of Parliament that
  prohibits, controls or regulates the importation or exportation of
  goods, release the copies of the work without further notice to the
  applicant if, within 10 working days after the applicant has been
  notified under subparagraph (3)(a)(ii), the applicant has not notified
  the Minister that they have commenced a proceeding for a final
  determination by the court of the issues referred to in paragraphs
  (1)(b) and (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If court finds in plaintiff's favour

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If, in a proceeding commenced under this section, the
  court is satisfied that the circumstances referred to in paragraphs
  (1)(b) and (c) existed, the court may make any order that it considers
  appropriate in the circumstances, including an order that the copies
  of the work be destroyed, or that they be delivered up to the
  plaintiff as the plaintiff's property absolutely.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other remedies not affected

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, nothing in this section
  affects any remedy available under any other provision of this Act or
  any other Act of Parliament.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 32, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Importation of books

- **[[44.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court may, subject to this section, make an order described in
  subsection 44.12(3) in relation to a book where the court is satisfied
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} copies of the book are about to be imported into
    Canada, or have been imported into Canada but have not yet been
    released;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} copies of the book were made with the consent of
    the owner of the copyright in the book in the country where the
    copies were made, but were imported without the consent of the owner
    in Canada of the copyright in the book; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the copies would infringe copyright if they were
    made in Canada by the importer and the importer knows or should have
    known this.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who may apply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A court may make an order described in subsection
  44.12(3) in relation to a book on application by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the owner of the copyright in the book in Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the exclusive licensee of the copyright in the book
    in Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the exclusive distributor of the book.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) only apply where there is an
  exclusive distributor of the book and the acts described in those
  subsections take place in the part of Canada or in respect of the
  particular sector of the market for which the person is the exclusive
  distributor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of certain provisions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 44.12(3) to (10) apply, with such
  modifications as the circumstances require, in respect of an order
  made under subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1994, c. 47, s. 66
- 1997, c. 24, s. 28
- 2014, c. 32, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44.2-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 44.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

**[[44.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.3 .sectionLabel}** No exclusive licensee
of the copyright in a book in Canada, and no exclusive distributor of a
book, may obtain an order under section 44.2 against another exclusive
licensee of the copyright in that book in Canada or against another
exclusive distributor of that book.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 28
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Importation of other subject-matter

**[[44.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44.4 .sectionLabel}** Section 44.12
applies, with such modifications as the circumstances require, in
respect of a sound recording, performer's performance or communication
signal, where a fixation or a reproduction of a fixation of it

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is about to be imported into Canada, or has been
  imported into Canada but has not yet been released;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} either

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} was made without the consent of the person who then
    owned the copyright in the sound recording, performer's performance
    or communication signal, as the case may be, in the country where
    the fixation or reproduction was made, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} was made elsewhere than in a country to which Part
    II extends; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} would infringe the right of the owner of copyright in
  the sound recording, performer's performance or communication signal
  if it was made in Canada by the importer and the importer knows or
  should have known this.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1997, c. 24, s. 28
- 2014, c. 32, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44.4-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 44.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

- **[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding anything in this
  Act, it is lawful for a person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to import for their own use not more than two
    copies of a work or other subject-matter made with the consent of
    the owner of the copyright in the country where it was made;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to import for use by a department of the Government
    of Canada or a province copies of a work or other subject-matter
    made with the consent of the owner of the copyright in the country
    where it was made;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at any time before copies of a work or other
    subject-matter are made in Canada, to import any copies, except
    copies of a book, made with the consent of the owner of the
    copyright in the country where the copies were made, that are
    required for the use of a library, archive, museum or educational
    institution;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to import, for the use of a library, archive,
    museum or educational institution, not more than one copy of a book
    that is made with the consent of the owner of the copyright in the
    country where the book was made; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to import copies, made with the consent of the
    owner of the copyright in the country where they were made, of any
    used books, except textbooks of a scientific, technical or scholarly
    nature for use within an educational institution in a course of
    instruction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Satisfactory evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer of customs may, in the officer's
  discretion, require a person seeking to import a copy of a work or
  other subject-matter under this section to produce satisfactory
  evidence of the facts necessary to establish the person's right to
  import the copy.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 45
- R.S., 1985, c. 41 (3rd Supp.), s. 117
- 1993, c. 44, s. 67
- 1994, c. 47, s. 67
- 1997, c. 24, s. 28
:::

## [PART V]{.HLabel1}[Administration]{.HTitleText1} {#h-104612 .Part}

### [Copyright Office]{.HTitleText2} {#h-104613 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright Office

**[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** The Copyright Office
shall be attached to the Patent Office.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. C-30, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Commissioner and Registrar

**[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner of
Patents shall exercise the powers conferred and perform the duties
imposed on him by this Act under the direction of the Minister, and, in
the absence of the Commissioner of Patents or if the Commissioner is
unable to act, the Registrar of Copyrights or other officer temporarily
appointed by the Minister may, as Acting Commissioner, exercise those
powers and perform those duties under the direction of the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. C-30, s. 30
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registrar

**[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** There shall be a
Registrar of Copyrights.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. C-30, s. 31
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Register of Copyrights, certificates and
certified copies

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner of
Patents, the Registrar of Copyrights or an officer, clerk or employee of
the Copyright Office may sign certificates and certified copies of the
Register of Copyrights.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 49
- 1992, c. 1, s. 47
- 1993, c. 15, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other duties of Registrar

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** The Registrar of
Copyrights shall perform such other duties in connection with the
administration of this Act as may be assigned to him by the Commissioner
of Patents.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. C-30, s. 33
:::

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1992, c. 1,
s. 48\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control of business and officials

**[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** The Commissioner of
Patents shall, subject to the Minister, oversee and direct the officers,
clerks and employees of the Copyright Office, have general control of
the business thereof and perform such other duties as are assigned to
him by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. C-30, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Register to be evidence

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Register of Copyrights is evidence of the particulars entered in it,
  and a copy of an entry in the Register is evidence of the particulars
  of the entry if it is certified by the Commissioner of Patents, the
  Registrar of Copyrights or an officer, clerk or employee of the
  Copyright Office as a true copy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Owner of copyright

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate of registration of copyright is
  evidence that the copyright subsists and that the person registered is
  the owner of the copyright.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignee

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A certificate of registration of an assignment of
  copyright is evidence that the right recorded on the certificate has
  been assigned and that the assignee registered is the owner of that
  right.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Licensee

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate of registration of a licence granting
  an interest in a copyright is evidence that the interest recorded on
  the certificate has been granted and that the licensee registered is
  the holder of that interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A certified copy or certificate appearing to have
  been issued under this section is admissible in all courts without
  proof of the signature or official character of the person appearing
  to have signed it.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 53
- 1992, c. 1, s. 49
- 1993, c. 15, s. 5
- 1997, c. 24, s. 30
:::

### [Registration]{.HTitleText2} {#h-104652 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Register of Copyrights

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall cause to be kept at the Copyright Office a register to
  be called the Register of Copyrights in which may be entered

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the names or titles of works and of other
    subject-matter in which copyright subsists;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the names and addresses of authors, performers,
    makers of sound recordings, broadcasters, owners of copyright,
    assignees of copyright, and persons to whom an interest in copyright
    has been granted by licence; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} such other particulars as may be prescribed by
    regulation.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1997, c. 24, s. 31\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single entry sufficient

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an encyclopaedia, newspaper, review,
  magazine or other periodical work, or work published in a series of
  books or parts, it is not necessary to make a separate entry for each
  number or part, but a single entry for the whole work is sufficient.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indices

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} There shall also be kept at the Copyright Office such
  indices of the Register established under this section as may be
  prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection and extracts

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Register and indices established under this
  section shall at all reasonable times be open to inspection, and any
  person is entitled to make copies of or take extracts from the
  Register.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former registration effective

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any registration made under the [Copyright
  Act](/eng/acts/C-42), chapter 70 of the Revised Statutes of Canada,
  1906, has the same force and effect as if made under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsisting copyright

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Any work in which copyright, operative in Canada,
  subsisted immediately before January 1, 1924 is registrable under this
  Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 54
- 1992, c. 1, s. 50
- 1997, c. 24, s. 31
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright in works

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Application for the registration of
  a copyright in a work may be made by or on behalf of the author of the
  work, the owner of the copyright in the work, an assignee of the
  copyright, or a person to whom an interest in the copyright has been
  granted by licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for registration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application under subsection (1) must be filed
  with the Copyright Office, be accompanied by the fee prescribed by or
  determined under the regulations, and contain the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the owner of the copyright
    in the work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a declaration that the applicant is the author of
    the work, the owner of the copyright in the work, an assignee of the
    copyright, or a person to whom an interest in the copyright has been
    granted by licence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the category of the work;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the title of the work;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the name of the author and, if the author is dead,
    the date of the author's death, if known;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a published work, the date and place
    of the first publication; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any additional information prescribed by
    regulation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-42, s. 55
- 1997, c. 24, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copyright in subject-matter other than
works

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Application for the registration of
  a copyright in subject-matter other than a work may be made by or on
  behalf of the owner of the copyright in the subject-matter, an
  assignee of the copyright, or a person to whom an interest in the
  copyright has been granted by licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for registration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application under subsection (1) must be filed
  with the Copyright Office, be accompanied by the fee prescribed by or
  determined under the regulations, and contain the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the owner of the copyright
    in the subject-matter;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a declaration that the applicant is the owner of
    the copyright in the subject-matter, an assignee of the copyright,
    or a person to whom an interest in the copyright has been granted by
    licence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether the subject-matter is a performer's
    performance, a sound recording or a communication signal;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the title, if any, of the subject-matter;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the date of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a performer's performance, its
